# Watchdog for Hung Programs (#38) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Detect a CMS program that is alive but hung and restart only that
program (or, in observe mode, say that it would), without restart storms,
with evidence before every action, and start again the programs that a
database outage left FATAL.

**Architecture:**
- **Part 1, service-side health:** a `health()` RPC on every asyncio
  service, busy-section tracking in ES, SS and PS (`cms/io/health.py`),
  new WorkerPool status fields, faulthandler on USR1, probe log demotion,
  the pool timeout as a constant, the supervisord group flags and
  `startretries=30`, the Checker removal and the `./status.sh` fix.
- **Part 2, the watchdog:** `cms/watchdog/` with a pure policy core
  (`policy.py`), probe adapters (`probes.py`), process sources and
  actuators (`control.py`) and the wiring (`main.py`, the `cmsWatchdog`
  script); its supervisord block and `.env` knobs; the operator
  documentation and the Caddy profile; the drill tooling and the harness
  flag.
- The enforce flip is an operator change in `.env`, made later (spec,
  Rollout). This plan ships observe mode as the default.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.12 (3.11+ syntax), asyncio, the standard library
(`xmlrpc.client` over a unix socket, `os.pidfd_open`,
`signal.pidfd_send_signal`, `faulthandler`), Tornado 6.5, SQLAlchemy 2.0
(one event listener), `psql` (PostgreSQL client), supervisord 4.2.5,
`unittest` with pytest, bash, Sphinx + MyST + sphinx-intl.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-watchdog-design.md` (the
binding authority; read it before any task). Where this plan and the spec
differ, the spec wins; tell the controller.

### Exact values (verbatim from the spec; `cms/watchdog/policy.py` holds them)

- **Cycle:** one asyncio loop on a monotonic 15 s schedule. Starved when
  the lag is over 5 s (no decisions; probes enter the windows as
  unknown). Paused when more than 30 s passed since the previous cycle
  ("paused for N s", every miss window cleared).
- **HTTP probe:** raw `asyncio.open_connection` to the configured listen
  address, `GET /static/favicon.ico HTTP/1.0`, 5 s, OK when the status
  line is 2xx or 3xx. A `0.0.0.0` or empty host is probed at
  `127.0.0.1`.
- **RPC probe:** the CMS JSON-RPC wire format, one persistent connection
  per service, `health` (plus `workers_status` on ES, on ES's
  connection), 3 s, OK when an answer with no error. On a timeout or
  error the connection is closed and reopened next cycle. On EOF,
  `ECONNRESET` or `EPIPE` before any byte of the answer on a reused
  connection, reconnect once at once within the same timeout.
- **Ranking probe:** `GET / HTTP/1.0` to the host and port of each
  `config.proxy_service.rankings` URL, 5 s, OK on any HTTP status line,
  no credentials. Alert only, after 3 failed cycles in a row.
- **DB gate:** one `psql -X -A -t -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1` subprocess per
  cycle; environment from `config.database.url` without the driver
  suffix: `PGHOST`, `PGPORT`, `PGUSER`, `PGPASSWORD` (percent-decoded,
  environment only, never argv), `PGDATABASE`, `sslmode` → `PGSSLMODE`,
  query `host` → `PGHOST`, `PGAPPNAME=cmsWatchdog`,
  `PGOPTIONS='-c statement_timeout=3000'`, `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT=3`; killed
  at 7 s. Green when it returns valid JSON; red with a reason classified
  from stderr: refused, timeout, too many clients, authentication, other.
- **R1** (CWS, AWS) and **R2** (ES, SS, PS, LogService, ResourceService):
  trip at 5 misses among the last 6 known results, the latest included
  (Task 9's review: a program that answers in a cycle is never restarted
  in it); early WARNING at 3, which says what happens at the trip in
  this mode and the holds that apply now.
- **R3 ES:** the oldest `busy` entry is a `post_finish_lock:*` section
  and (a) `age_s` > 180 and `idle_s` ≥ 60; (b) the holder thread's
  utime+stime grew by less than 1 s since the newest sample at least 60
  s old (samples kept 90 s); (c) no backend with
  `application_name = 'cmsEvaluationService 0'` in state `active` whose
  `wait_event_type` is not `Lock` and whose `wait_event` is not
  `ClientWrite` (NULL counts as "not"). WARNING at `age_s` > 90 and
  `idle_s` ≥ 30. The invalidation holder
  (`post_finish_lock:_invalidate_submission_sync`) never trips; past 180
  s it alerts "re-evaluation still running for N min" every 5 minutes.
- **R3 SS:** the `score` section's `age_s` > 180 (WARNING > 90).
  **R3 PS:** a `send` section's `idle_s` > 180 and every ranking answers
  its probe (WARNING `idle_s` > 90).
- **R4 Worker:** (a) ES reports `disabled_by_timeout`,
  now − `disabled_at` ≥ 180 s, and the process started before
  `disabled_at`; or (b) RUNNING ≥ 300 s, ES up ≥ 120 s, at least one
  other Worker connected, this one not.
- **R5 FATAL:** supervisord reports FATAL, the gate was red at some time
  in [last_start − 15 s, now), and it has been green for 2 cycles since.
  One action starts every eligible FATAL program, in class order
  infra → core → web → workers.
- **Holds, in this order:** H1 supervisord did not answer; H2 gate red,
  or green for less than 120 s after a red the watchdog saw (its own
  start is H6's); H3 a backend whose `application_name`
  starts with `cms`, `wait_event_type = 'Lock'`, statement older than 30
  s; H4 2 or more programs of the trip's class failing (≥ 3 misses among
  the last 6 known results, or a section past half its trip; over-budget
  programs do not count; Workers: 2 or more R4 trips, one alert); H5 more
  than 1/3 of the programs probed over HTTP or RPC failed any probe in
  this cycle (Workers and rankings not counted); H6 program started less
  than 120 s ago, its last would-action less than 120 s ago, or the
  watchdog started less than 120 s ago; H7 2 restarts of the program or
  3 in its pool (Workers pool; web+core+infra pool) in the last 60
  minutes, FATAL starts 2 per program per 60 minutes, an unparsable
  ledger exhausts every budget for 60 minutes; H8 another action in
  flight; H9 enforce mode and the class not in `CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART`
  (a would-action booked in the shadow ledger, within H7's limits).
  R5 obeys H1, H2 (its own 2 green cycles), H6 (the watchdog's own
  startup only), H7 (the FATAL budget), H8 and H9.
- **Actions:** book (ledger + journal) before any signal; `pidfd_open`,
  re-read the source, refuse unless still RUNNING with that pid;
  evidence and USR1 (leader only, `pidfd_send_signal`, only if `SigCgt`
  bit 10 is set, the kind is on the allow-list and State is not `T`;
  wait 1 s); `killpg(pid, SIGTERM)`; wait up to 10 s for the pidfd;
  `killpg(pid, SIGKILL)` (a `ProcessLookupError` means the group is
  empty); confirm a new pid RUNNING within 30 s; "did it help" at the
  first pass, or "still failing" at 120 s. A start batch calls
  `supervisor.startProcess(name, False)` per program; `ALREADY_STARTED`
  counts as success. Never `supervisorctl restart`, `stop` or `start`.
- **Alerts:** stdout always; LogService through one `Log` RPC on a
  persistent connection of its own, `service_name="Watchdog"`,
  `service_shard=0`, WARNING or ERROR, best effort with a 2 s timeout;
  open alerts sent again when LogService gets a new pid. Rate limit: one
  line per (program, rule) per 5 minutes while open; state changes are
  not rate-limited. Within a hold, a new headline is rate-limited too,
  except the first H7 or H3 of the hold episode (Task 9's review:
  `held_headline`, `held_announced`, `URGENT_HOLDS`; a pid change, a
  restart or a start ends the episode). Hourly near-miss summary at
  INFO.
- **Self-guard:** `faulthandler.dump_traceback_later(60, exit=True)`
  re-armed every cycle.
- **Files:** `run/watchdog-ledger.json` and `run/watchdog.json` in
  `config.global_.run_dir`; `log/watchdog/actions.jsonl` and
  `log/watchdog/<UTC timestamp>-<program>.json` in
  `config.global_.log_dir`. Every write is a temporary file, then a
  rename (the journal is appended).
- **Knobs:** `CMS_WATCHDOG=off|observe|enforce` (default `observe`; `off`
  writes no program block); `CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART`, a comma-separated
  subset of `web,core,infra,workers` (default `web,core,infra`). Both
  validated by `docker/generate_config.py`: an invalid value exits.
- **supervisord:** every program block gets `stopasgroup=true`,
  `killasgroup=true` and `startretries=30`; `startsecs` stays at 1; CWS
  keeps `stopwaitsecs=40`; `[program:cmswatchdog]` has `priority=90`,
  `stopwaitsecs=5`, the group flags and `startretries=30`.
- **Classes:** `web` = every CWS plus AWS; `core` = ES, SS, PS; `infra`
  = LogService, ResourceService; `workers` = every Worker. Managed kinds:
  LogService, ResourceService, EvaluationService, ScoringService,
  ProxyService, ContestWebServer, AdminWebServer, Worker. Everything else
  (the loader, TelegramBot, the watchdog, unknown commands) is never
  acted on.
- **Layering:** `policy.py` imports only the standard library; `probes.py`
  the standard library and `cms.config`; `control.py` the standard
  library; `main.py` those three and `cms.config`. `procfs.py`, a private
  helper that imports only the standard library, holds the one copy of
  `CLOCK_TICKS` and `stat_fields` that `probes.py` and `control.py` both
  import (importing one from the other would break this layering). The
  watchdog is not an `AsyncService`, has no `[services]` entry, and does
  not use `AsyncRemoteServiceClient` or the `cms.log` handlers.

## Review Focus

The five inputs or conditions the spec implies but does not test, the most
likely to bite first, each pinned by a test in the task that owns the code:

1. **`.env` knobs written loosely:** `CMS_WATCHDOG=Enforce`,
   `CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART=Web, core`, a duplicate class, a trailing comma,
   or an empty `CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART=` meant as "nothing". Case and spaces
   are normalized, duplicates dropped, an empty value means the default
   (documented in `.env.example`: to restart nothing, keep observe mode),
   and anything else exits with the variable's name. Tests:
   `test_watchdog_knobs_are_normalized` and
   `test_watchdog_restart_empty_means_the_default` (Task 18).
2. **A supervisord program whose command the watchdog cannot read** (a
   block added by hand while `getAllConfigInfo` lacks it, an empty or
   unparsable `command=`, an unbalanced quote). It must be unmanaged,
   listed in the status file, never acted on, and never crash the cycle.
   Tests: `test_a_program_without_config_info_is_unmanaged` and
   `test_an_unparsable_command_is_unmanaged` (Task 14).
3. **The state directories missing or not writable** (a fresh `cms-logs`
   volume without `watchdog/`, a full disk, `run/` removed by hand). The
   watchdog creates `log/watchdog/`, keeps cycling, and alerts (rate
   limited) when the status file or the journal cannot be written; only a
   failed ledger write stops an action. Tests:
   `test_an_unwritable_status_file_alerts_and_the_cycle_goes_on` and
   `test_the_evidence_directory_is_created` (Task 17).
4. **A malformed `health()` payload** (`{}`, `busy` not a list, an entry
   without `idle_s`, a non-numeric `age_s`, a missing `pid`). It counts as
   "no sections" and no pid check, and `decide()` never raises. Test:
   `test_a_malformed_health_payload_is_no_section` (Task 10).
5. **`cmsWatchdog --snapshot` of a program that does not exist or is not
   RUNNING** (a typo, `cmsworker9` on a 4-Worker deployment, a STOPPED
   CWS). It prints what it found and exits 1, with nothing signalled and
   nothing booked. Test: `test_snapshot_of_an_unknown_program_exits_1`
   (Task 17).

### Decisions this plan takes, and why

- **`decide()` keeps the spec's signature** `decide(state, observation,
  mono, wall) -> (state, decisions)`. It deep-copies the state, so the
  caller's object is never changed. Alerts are decisions too
  (`action == "alert"`), so one list carries everything a cycle emits.
- **The policy owns the alert rate limit** (pure, fake-clock tested), and
  `main.py` owns the channels and the resend on a new LogService pid.
- **`POOL_TIMEOUT_S` is defined in `cms/db/connect_options.py`** (which
  both engine modules already import, with no circular import) and
  imported into `cms.db`, so that `cms.db.POOL_TIMEOUT_S` exists as the
  spec names it.
- **SS and PS wrap their sections without re-indenting the bodies:**
  `_execute_sync` becomes a three-line wrapper around the old body,
  renamed `_score_sync` / `_send_batch_sync`. The hunk in
  `ScoringService.py` (shared with #11) stays small, and every existing
  test that calls `_execute_sync` still works. The new `busy` argument of
  both executors defaults to a private tracker, so the existing tests
  that build executors without it keep working.
- **`getAllConfigInfo()` was checked against supervisor 4.2.5**, the
  package of `ubuntu:noble` and `debian:bookworm` (the image's bases):
  it returns `command` for every process (and `ALREADY_STARTED` is fault
  code 60). No fallback parser of `supervisord.conf` is written; a
  program without config info is unmanaged (Review Focus 2). Rig P
  confirms it on the image (Validation, step P0).
- **psql runs with `LC_ALL=C`:** its messages are localized otherwise
  (checked: "falló la conexión" on a Spanish host), and the red reason is
  classified from them.
- **The supervisord test server needs a handler with
  `disable_nagle_algorithm = False`:** `SimpleXMLRPCRequestHandler` sets
  `TCP_NODELAY`, which a unix socket refuses (checked).
- **The harness needs one status record per cycle**, so `cmsWatchdog` has
  a `--status-history PATH` option (append each cycle's status as one
  JSON line), used only by `run.sh --watchdog`.
- **USR1 is sent by the actuator** (it holds the verified pidfd), after
  `main.py`'s evidence writer has written the file.
- **CWS and AWS are probed with `health()` too** ("`health()` of each
  asyncio service", spec 3.3): it feeds the pid check, the evidence and
  H5, while R1 judges them on the favicon only.
- **H6's "last would-action" grace also follows a real action**
  (Task 9). In enforce mode the restarted program's new start time
  already gives that grace; counting the action itself too keeps observe
  and enforce identical on the same observations, which the invariant
  test checks.
- **R4 (b) does not apply to a Worker that ES disabled less than 180 s
  ago** (Task 10): ES's `quit` makes it disconnect at once, and (b)
  would otherwise restart it before (a)'s 180 s, which exist so that
  ES's own `quit` has ended first.
- **A 404 of the favicon is logged at WARNING, as Tornado does today**
  (Task 6). The spec's Testing section says "a 404 or any other path at
  INFO", but its Design (2.5, binding) says "every other request is
  logged as today".
- **R5 in observe mode re-trips every cycle** until its FATAL budget
  (2 would-starts, then H7): the spec exempts R5 from the program part
  of H6, and in observe mode the program stays FATAL. This plan keeps
  the spec's rule.
- **A program the `/proc` source loses (ABSENT) gets an alert, nothing
  else** (Task 11), as spec 3.2 says.
- **The harness's "from the driver's start" is the first request of
  `requests.jsonl`** (Task 21).
- **Tasks 10-21 follow `policy.py` as Task 9's review left it** (four
  fix rounds after this plan was first written). So R3's early warnings
  go through Task 9's `_early_warnings` and `_outlook`, like R1's and
  R2's, and say when R3 trips (Task 10). A held R5 start goes through
  `_held` as a trip of rule R5, so it gets the same line, `_other_holds`
  and rate limits (Task 11). The gate parser drops a backend row with
  an age that is not a finite number rather than reject the whole
  snapshot, which would turn the gate red and hold everything (Task 13).
- **The code of this plan was checked before it was written down:** the
  policy, probes, control, main, docker, loadtest and Part 1 code, and
  their tests, were assembled from this document into a scratch copy
  outside the repository and run with the worktree's venv (all pass,
  pyflakes and pycodestyle clean, the policy also at the stages of Tasks
  9 and 10); the documentation edits and the three translation tables
  passed `docs/check_translations.py` and both Sphinx builds with `-W`.
  Tasks 10-21 were checked again the same way over the implemented and
  reviewed Tasks 1-9 (c7ffd67f), their documentation included. If a
  step still fails, the cause is a difference between this branch and
  the code the plan read (2c45f0c8 plus the spec commits, then
  c7ffd67f for Tasks 10-21): report it.

```python
# (fork header)

"""Fakes shared by the tests of the busy sections and health() (#38)."""


class FakeClock:
    """A monotonic clock that moves only when a test moves it."""

    def __init__(self, now: float = 1000.0):
        self.now = now

    def __call__(self) -> float:
        return self.now
```

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for cms.io.health: busy sections and the USR1 stack dump."""

import signal
import subprocess
import sys
import textwrap
import threading
import time
import unittest

from sqlalchemy import create_engine, text

from cms.io.health import STACK_FRAMES, BusyTracker
from cmstestsuite.unit_tests.healthfakes import FakeClock


class TestBusyTracker(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.clock = FakeClock()
        self.tracker = BusyTracker(clock=self.clock)
        self.release = threading.Event()
        self.addCleanup(self.release.set)

    def _hold(self, what: str) -> tuple[threading.Thread, list[int]]:
        """Start a thread that holds the section what until release.

        return: the thread, and a list that gets its native id.

        """
        entered = threading.Event()
        tids: list[int] = []

        def holder_section():
            tids.append(threading.get_native_id())
            with self.tracker.busy(what):
                entered.set()
                self.release.wait(10)

        thread = threading.Thread(target=holder_section, daemon=True)
        thread.start()
        self.assertTrue(entered.wait(5))
        return thread, tids

    def test_a_section_belongs_to_its_thread(self):
        thread, tids = self._hold("score")

        # Another thread's stop() does not end it.
        self.tracker.stop()
        report = self.tracker.report()

        self.assertEqual([entry["what"] for entry in report], ["score"])
        self.assertEqual(report[0]["tid"], tids[0])
        self.assertNotEqual(report[0]["tid"], threading.get_native_id())
        self.release.set()
        thread.join(5)
        self.assertEqual(self.tracker.report(), [])

    def test_the_report_lists_the_oldest_first(self):
        older, _ = self._hold("older")
        self.clock.now += 5
        newer, _ = self._hold("newer")
        self.clock.now += 1

        report = self.tracker.report()

        self.assertEqual([entry["what"] for entry in report],
                         ["older", "newer"])
        self.assertEqual([entry["age_s"] for entry in report], [6.0, 1.0])
        self.release.set()
        older.join(5)
        newer.join(5)

    def test_the_stack_comes_only_after_its_threshold(self):
        thread, _ = self._hold("post_finish_lock:x")

        self.clock.now += 29
        self.assertIsNone(self.tracker.report(stack_after_s=30)[0]["stack"])
        self.clock.now += 1
        stack = self.tracker.report(stack_after_s=30)[0]["stack"]

        self.assertIn("holder_section", stack)
        self.assertLessEqual(stack.count('File "'), STACK_FRAMES)
        self.release.set()
        thread.join(5)

    def test_idle_follows_the_steps(self):
        self.tracker.start("send")
        self.clock.now += 10
        self.assertEqual(self.tracker.report()[0]["idle_s"], 10)

        self.tracker.step()
        self.clock.now += 4
        entry = self.tracker.report()[0]

        self.assertEqual(
            (entry["statements"], entry["idle_s"], entry["age_s"]),
            (1, 4, 14))
        self.tracker.stop()

    def test_a_step_outside_a_section_does_nothing(self):
        self.tracker.step()
        self.assertEqual(self.tracker.report(), [])

    def test_start_replaces_a_stale_record_of_its_thread(self):
        self.tracker.start("stale")
        self.clock.now += 50
        self.tracker.start("fresh")

        self.assertEqual(
            [(entry["what"], entry["age_s"])
             for entry in self.tracker.report()],
            [("fresh", 0)])
        self.tracker.stop()
        self.assertEqual(self.tracker.report(), [])

    def test_the_section_ends_when_its_body_raises(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            with self.tracker.busy("score"):
                raise ValueError("boom")
        self.assertEqual(self.tracker.report(), [])

    def test_concurrent_starts_and_stops(self):
        tracker = BusyTracker()
        errors: list[Exception] = []

        def churn(index: int):
            try:
                for _ in range(500):
                    with tracker.busy("section %d" % index):
                        tracker.step()
            except Exception as error:
                errors.append(error)

        threads = [threading.Thread(target=churn, args=(index,))
                   for index in range(8)]
        for thread in threads:
            thread.start()
        while any(thread.is_alive() for thread in threads):
            for entry in tracker.report(stack_after_s=0):
                self.assertTrue(entry["what"].startswith("section "))
        for thread in threads:
            thread.join(10)

        self.assertEqual(errors, [])
        self.assertEqual(tracker.report(), [])

    def test_the_statements_of_an_engine_are_steps_of_their_thread(self):
        engine = create_engine("sqlite://")
        self.addCleanup(engine.dispose)
        self.tracker.count_statements_of(engine)

        with engine.connect() as connection:
            # Outside a section: not counted.
            connection.execute(text("SELECT 1"))
            self.tracker.start("post_finish_lock:x")
            self.clock.now += 3
            connection.execute(text("SELECT 1"))
            connection.execute(text("SELECT 2"))
            entry = self.tracker.report()[0]
            self.tracker.stop()

        self.assertEqual(entry["statements"], 2)
        self.assertEqual(entry["idle_s"], 0)


class TestRegisterStackDump(unittest.TestCase):

    def test_usr1_dumps_the_stacks_and_the_process_lives_on(self):
        script = textwrap.dedent("""\
            import time
            from cms.io.health import register_stack_dump
            register_stack_dump()
            print("ready", flush=True)
            time.sleep(60)
            """)
        process = subprocess.Popen(
            [sys.executable, "-c", script], stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
            stderr=subprocess.PIPE, text=True)
        try:
            self.assertEqual(process.stdout.readline().strip(), "ready")
            process.send_signal(signal.SIGUSR1)
            time.sleep(0.5)
            self.assertIsNone(process.poll())
        finally:
            process.terminate()
            _, stderr = process.communicate(timeout=10)
        self.assertIn("Current thread", stderr)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the module.** Create `cms/io/health.py`:

```python
# (fork header)

"""Health reporting of a CMS service: busy sections and stack dumps.

A service marks the parts of its work that must never hang (a locked
section of EvaluationService, an execution of ScoringService or
ProxyService) with a BusyTracker, and its health() RPC reports them:
their age, how long ago they last made progress and, once old enough,
the stack of the thread that holds them. cmsWatchdog (#38) reads that
to tell a slow section from a stuck one.

This module imports only the standard library at module level:
BusyTracker.count_statements_of imports sqlalchemy.event when called.

"""

import contextlib
import faulthandler
import signal
import sys
import threading
import time
import traceback
from collections.abc import Callable, Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any


# How many frames of the holder's stack report() gives (the innermost).
STACK_FRAMES = 30


@dataclass
class _Section:
    """One thread's current section (see BusyTracker)."""

    what: str
    tid: int
    start: float
    statements: int
    last_statement: float


class BusyTracker:
    """Track the sections of a service that must not hang, per thread.

    A thread starts a section, may count steps of progress in it (a
    database statement, an HTTP request) and stops it. Each thread
    changes only its own record, and report() works on a copy of the
    records (list(dict.items()) is atomic under the GIL), so there is no
    lock: report() never waits for a section, whatever the section
    holds.

    """

    def __init__(self, clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic):
        """Create a tracker with no section.

        clock: the monotonic clock to measure with (tests pass a fake).

        """
        self._clock = clock
        # By threading.get_ident(), the key of sys._current_frames().
        self._sections: dict[int, _Section] = {}

    @contextlib.contextmanager
    def busy(self, what: str) -> Iterator[None]:
        """Run the body of a with statement as the section what.

        what: the name of the section, as report() gives it.

        """
        self.start(what)
        try:
            yield
        finally:
            self.stop()

    def start(self, what: str) -> None:
        """Start a section in the current thread.

        A record the thread left behind (a section never stopped) is
        replaced.

        what: the name of the section, as report() gives it.

        """
        now = self._clock()
        self._sections[threading.get_ident()] = _Section(
            what, threading.get_native_id(), now, 0, now)

    def stop(self) -> None:
        """End the current thread's section, if it has one."""
        self._sections.pop(threading.get_ident(), None)

    def step(self) -> None:
        """Count one step of progress in the current thread's section.

        Outside a section it does nothing.

        """
        section = self._sections.get(threading.get_ident())
        if section is not None:
            section.statements += 1
            section.last_statement = self._clock()

    def count_statements_of(self, engine: Any) -> None:
        """Count every statement that engine runs as a step.

        A statement counts for the thread that runs it, so only the
        statements of a thread inside a section count.

        engine: a SQLAlchemy Engine.

        """
        from sqlalchemy import event
        event.listen(engine, "before_cursor_execute", self._on_statement)

    def _on_statement(self, *args: Any) -> None:
        """Count a statement (SQLAlchemy's before_cursor_execute)."""
        self.step()

    def report(self, stack_after_s: float = 30.0) -> list[dict]:
        """Describe the sections in progress, the oldest first.

        stack_after_s: from which age an entry carries the stack of its
            thread.

        return: one dict per section: "what"; "tid", the holder's
            native thread id (as in /proc/<pid>/task/); "age_s";
            "statements", the steps counted; "idle_s", the seconds
            since the last step, or since the start without one; and
            "stack", the innermost STACK_FRAMES frames of the holder as
            text, or None while the section is younger than
            stack_after_s.

        """
        now = self._clock()
        sections = sorted(list(self._sections.items()),
                          key=lambda item: item[1].start)
        frames: dict[int, Any] | None = None
        result = []
        for ident, section in sections:
            age = now - section.start
            stack = None
            if age >= stack_after_s:
                if frames is None:
                    frames = sys._current_frames()
                frame = frames.get(ident)
                if frame is not None:
                    stack = "".join(
                        traceback.format_stack(frame)[-STACK_FRAMES:])
            result.append({
                "what": section.what,
                "tid": section.tid,
                "age_s": age,
                "statements": section.statements,
                "idle_s": now - section.last_statement,
                "stack": stack,
            })
        return result


def register_stack_dump() -> None:
    """Make SIGUSR1 write the stack of every thread to stderr.

    The process goes on running: faulthandler writes the stacks and
    returns. cmsWatchdog (#38) sends SIGUSR1 to a program before it
    restarts it, so that ./logs.sh shows where it was stuck. Call it
    from the method that runs a service, never from __init__: a service
    built in a test must not change the test process.

    """
    faulthandler.register(signal.SIGUSR1, file=sys.__stderr__,
                          all_threads=True)
```

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for AsyncService.health() and the USR1 stack dump (#38)."""

import asyncio
import json
import os
import signal
import subprocess
import sys
import textwrap
import threading
import time
import unittest
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch

from cms.conf import Address
from cms.io.async_service import AsyncService
from cms.io.service import Service
from cmstestsuite.unit_tests.servicelogmixin import \
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin


class QueueService(AsyncService):
    """An AsyncService with a field of its own in health()."""

    def _health_extra(self) -> dict:
        return {"queue": 7}


def _read_line(stream, timeout: float) -> str:
    """Return the next line of stream, or "" after timeout seconds."""
    lines: list[str] = []
    reader = threading.Thread(
        target=lambda: lines.append(stream.readline()), daemon=True)
    reader.start()
    reader.join(timeout)
    return lines[0] if lines else ""


@patch("cms.io.async_service.get_service_address",
       return_value=Address("127.0.0.1", 0))
class TestHealth(
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin, unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase,
):

    async def test_health_reports_the_pid_the_uptime_and_the_sections(
            self, _address):
        service = QueueService(0)
        service._busy.start("score")
        self.addCleanup(service._busy.stop)

        answer = service.health()

        self.assertEqual(answer["pid"], os.getpid())
        self.assertGreaterEqual(answer["uptime_s"], 0)
        self.assertEqual([entry["what"] for entry in answer["busy"]],
                         ["score"])
        self.assertEqual(answer["queue"], 7)

    async def test_the_default_extra_fields_are_none(self, _address):
        answer = AsyncService(0).health()
        self.assertEqual(set(answer), {"pid", "uptime_s", "busy"})
        self.assertEqual(answer["busy"], [])

    async def test_health_is_an_rpc_method_answered_by_the_loop(
            self, _address):
        service = QueueService(0)
        run_task = asyncio.create_task(service._async_run())
        self.addAsyncCleanup(self._stop, service, run_task)
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        port = service._server.sockets[0].getsockname()[1]

        reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", port)
        try:
            request = {"__id": "1", "__method": "health", "__data": {}}
            writer.write(json.dumps(request).encode() + b"\r\n")
            await writer.drain()
            line = await asyncio.wait_for(reader.readuntil(b"\r\n"), 5)
        finally:
            writer.close()

        response = json.loads(line)
        self.assertIsNone(response["__error"])
        self.assertEqual(response["__data"]["pid"], os.getpid())
        self.assertEqual(response["__data"]["queue"], 7)

    async def test_building_a_service_registers_no_stack_dump(
            self, _address):
        with patch("cms.io.async_service.register_stack_dump") as register:
            AsyncService(0)
        register.assert_not_called()

    async def test_running_a_service_registers_the_stack_dump(
            self, _address):
        service = AsyncService(0)
        with patch("cms.io.async_service.register_stack_dump") as register:
            run_task = asyncio.create_task(service._async_run())
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
            await self._stop(service, run_task)
        register.assert_called_once_with()

    @staticmethod
    async def _stop(service: AsyncService, run_task: asyncio.Task):
        if not run_task.done():
            service.exit()
            await asyncio.wait_for(run_task, timeout=5)


class TestGeventServiceStackDump(unittest.TestCase):

    def test_run_registers_the_stack_dump(self):
        # Not a real Service: building one installs signal handlers in
        # the test process. run() only needs these attributes.
        fake = MagicMock()
        fake._my_coord = ("Worker", 0)
        with patch("cms.io.service.register_stack_dump") as register, \
                patch("cms.io.service.config") as config:
            config.global_.backdoor = False
            self.assertTrue(Service.run(fake))
        register.assert_called_once_with()
        fake.rpc_server.serve_forever.assert_called_once_with()


class TestWebServiceStackDump(unittest.TestCase):
    """A real WebService, run in its own process, survives USR1."""

    def test_usr1_dumps_the_stacks_of_a_running_web_service(self):
        script = textwrap.dedent("""\
            from unittest.mock import patch
            from cms.conf import Address
            from cms.io.web_service import WebService

            with patch("cms.io.async_service.get_service_address",
                       return_value=Address("127.0.0.1", 0)):
                service = WebService(0, [], {}, shard=0,
                                     listen_address="127.0.0.1")
            service._call_when_running(
                lambda: print("running", flush=True))
            service.run()
            """)
        process = subprocess.Popen(
            [sys.executable, "-c", script], stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
            stderr=subprocess.PIPE, text=True)
        try:
            self.assertEqual(_read_line(process.stdout, 30).strip(),
                             "running")
            process.send_signal(signal.SIGUSR1)
            time.sleep(1)
            self.assertIsNone(process.poll())
            process.send_signal(signal.SIGTERM)
            _, stderr = process.communicate(timeout=30)
        finally:
            if process.poll() is None:
                process.kill()
                process.communicate()
        self.assertIn("Current thread", stderr)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```

```python
    @rpc_method
    def health(self) -> dict:
        """Report that the event loop is free, and what is in progress.

        It runs on the event loop, so any answer at all proves that the
        loop is not blocked. cmsWatchdog calls it every 15 seconds
        (#38).

        return: "pid", the process id; "uptime_s", the seconds since
            the service was built; "busy", the sections in progress
            (see BusyTracker.report), the oldest first; and the fields
            of _health_extra().

        """
        return {
            "pid": os.getpid(),
            "uptime_s": time.monotonic() - self._started_at,
            "busy": self._busy.report(),
            **self._health_extra(),
        }

    def _health_extra(self) -> dict:
        """Return the service's own fields for health(): none here.

        return: a dict merged into health()'s answer.

        """
        return {}
```

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for EvaluationService's busy section in health() (#38).

The sections block on threading.Event objects and the invalidation
stops at its first SessionGen, so only the statement-counting test
reaches the database.

This file is asyncio-only: it must never share a process with the
gevent-based twophase files (see docker/_cms-test-internal.sh).

"""

import asyncio
import contextlib
import threading
import time
import unittest
from unittest.mock import patch

from sqlalchemy import text

from cms.conf import Address
from cms.db import SessionGen
from cms.io.health import BusyTracker
from cms.service.EvaluationService import EvaluationService, \
    with_post_finish_lock
from cmstestsuite.unit_tests.healthfakes import FakeClock, StopHere
from cmstestsuite.unit_tests.servicelogmixin import \
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin


INVALIDATION_SECTION = "post_finish_lock:_invalidate_submission_sync"


class SectionService(EvaluationService):
    """EvaluationService with sections a test can hold and release."""

    @with_post_finish_lock
    def _blocking_section_sync(self, entered: threading.Event,
                               release: threading.Event,
                               tids: list[int]) -> None:
        tids.append(threading.get_native_id())
        entered.set()
        release.wait(10)

    @with_post_finish_lock
    def _waiting_section_sync(self) -> str:
        return "done"

    @with_post_finish_lock
    def _outer_section_sync(self) -> list[dict]:
        return self._inner_section_sync()

    @with_post_finish_lock
    def _inner_section_sync(self) -> list[dict]:
        return self._busy.report()

    @with_post_finish_lock
    def _query_section_sync(self) -> list[dict]:
        with SessionGen() as session:
            session.execute(text("SELECT 1"))
        return self._busy.report()


class TestEvaluationServiceHealth(
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin, unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase,
):

    async def asyncSetUp(self):
        # As in evaluationservice_loop_actions_test.py: no Worker,
        # LogService or ScoringService is reachable.
        local_address = Address("127.0.0.1", 0)
        for patcher in (
            patch("cms.util.config.services", {}),
            patch("cms.io.async_service.get_service_address",
                  return_value=local_address),
            patch("cms.io.async_rpc.get_service_address",
                  return_value=local_address),
        ):
            patcher.start()
            self.addCleanup(patcher.stop)
        with patch.object(
                EvaluationService, "start_sweeper",
                lambda self, timeout: None):
            self.service = SectionService(0)
        self.loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
        self.service._loop = self.loop
        self.addCleanup(self.service._disconnect_all)
        self.release = threading.Event()
        self.addCleanup(self.release.set)

    async def asyncTearDown(self):
        for task in list(self.service._background_tasks):
            task.cancel()

    async def _wait(self, event: threading.Event):
        deadline = time.monotonic() + 5
        while not event.is_set():
            self.assertLess(time.monotonic(), deadline,
                            "the section did not start")
            await asyncio.sleep(0.01)

    async def test_health_names_the_holder_its_age_thread_and_stack(self):
        clock = FakeClock()
        self.service._busy = BusyTracker(clock=clock)
        entered, tids = threading.Event(), []
        section = self.loop.run_in_executor(
            None, self.service._blocking_section_sync, entered,
            self.release, tids)
        await self._wait(entered)
        clock.now += 31

        answer = self.service.health()

        self.assertEqual(len(answer["busy"]), 1)
        entry = answer["busy"][0]
        self.assertEqual(entry["what"],
                         "post_finish_lock:_blocking_section_sync")
        self.assertEqual(entry["age_s"], 31)
        self.assertEqual(entry["tid"], tids[0])
        self.assertIn("_blocking_section_sync", entry["stack"])
        self.assertEqual(answer["queue"], 0)
        self.release.set()
        await asyncio.wait_for(section, 5)
        self.assertEqual(self.service.health()["busy"], [])

    async def test_a_thread_waiting_for_the_lock_is_not_listed(self):
        entered, tids = threading.Event(), []
        holder = self.loop.run_in_executor(
            None, self.service._blocking_section_sync, entered,
            self.release, tids)
        await self._wait(entered)
        waiter = self.loop.run_in_executor(
            None, self.service._waiting_section_sync)
        await asyncio.sleep(0.2)

        self.assertFalse(waiter.done())
        self.assertEqual(
            [entry["what"] for entry in self.service.health()["busy"]],
            ["post_finish_lock:_blocking_section_sync"])
        self.release.set()
        self.assertEqual(await asyncio.wait_for(waiter, 5), "done")
        await asyncio.wait_for(holder, 5)
        self.assertEqual(self.service.health()["busy"], [])

    async def test_nested_calls_are_one_section_named_by_the_outermost(
            self):
        report = await self.loop.run_in_executor(
            None, self.service._outer_section_sync)
        self.assertEqual([entry["what"] for entry in report],
                         ["post_finish_lock:_outer_section_sync"])

    async def test_the_section_ends_even_when_the_flush_raises(self):
        with patch.object(self.service, "_flush_loop_actions",
                          side_effect=RuntimeError("boom")):
            with self.assertRaises(RuntimeError):
                await self.loop.run_in_executor(
                    None, self.service._waiting_section_sync)
        self.assertEqual(self.service.health()["busy"], [])

    async def test_an_invalidation_is_the_section_the_watchdog_spares(
            self):
        entered = threading.Event()

        @contextlib.contextmanager
        def blocked_session():
            entered.set()
            self.release.wait(10)
            raise StopHere()
            yield  # Unreachable: it makes this a generator.

        with patch("cms.service.EvaluationService.SessionGen",
                   blocked_session):
            invalidation = asyncio.ensure_future(
                self.service.invalidate_submission(level="compilation"))
            await self._wait(entered)
            busy = self.service.health()["busy"]
            self.release.set()
            with self.assertRaises(StopHere):
                await asyncio.wait_for(invalidation, 5)

        self.assertEqual([entry["what"] for entry in busy],
                         [INVALIDATION_SECTION])
        self.assertEqual(self.service.health()["busy"], [])

    async def test_the_statements_of_a_section_are_counted(self):
        report = await self.loop.run_in_executor(
            None, self.service._query_section_sync)
        self.assertEqual(len(report), 1)
        self.assertGreaterEqual(report[0]["statements"], 1)

    def test_the_sweeper_period_is_a_constant(self):
        self.assertEqual(EvaluationService.SWEEPER_PERIOD_S, 117.0)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```

```python
# (fork header)

"""Fixtures shared by the tests of cms/service.

This file is loaded by both test groups (see
docker/_cms-test-internal.sh): it must not import cmscontrib or
cmsranking, nor patch anything with gevent.

"""

from collections.abc import Iterator
from typing import Any

import pytest
from sqlalchemy import event

from cms.io.health import BusyTracker


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def remove_statement_listeners(
        monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> Iterator[None]:
    """Remove, after each test, the engine listeners it added.

    EvaluationService.__init__ makes its BusyTracker count the
    statements of cms.db.engine (#38). Production builds one
    EvaluationService per process; a test process builds dozens, and
    each listener would stay on the engine and count every later
    statement of the process.

    monkeypatch: pytest's fixture, to wrap count_statements_of.

    """
    added: list[tuple[BusyTracker, Any]] = []
    count_statements_of = BusyTracker.count_statements_of

    def counting(tracker: BusyTracker, engine: Any) -> None:
        count_statements_of(tracker, engine)
        added.append((tracker, engine))

    monkeypatch.setattr(BusyTracker, "count_statements_of", counting)
    yield
    for tracker, engine in added:
        event.remove(engine, "before_cursor_execute", tracker._on_statement)
```

```python
def with_post_finish_lock(func):
    """Decorator for locking on self.post_finish_lock.

    Ensures that no more than one decorated function is executing at
    the same time. The outermost decorated call is a "section": when it
    ends (normally or with an exception), the loop actions it scheduled
    are handed to the event loop, still under the lock. Nested calls
    leave them to the outermost one. health() reports the section as
    "post_finish_lock:<function name>" (see cms.io.health) until its
    loop actions are handed over; threads waiting for the lock are not
    reported.

    """
    @wraps(func)
    def wrapped(self, *args, **kwargs):
        with self.post_finish_lock:
            self._post_finish_depth += 1
            if self._post_finish_depth == 1:
                self._busy.start("post_finish_lock:" + func.__name__)
            try:
                return func(self, *args, **kwargs)
            finally:
                self._post_finish_depth -= 1
                if self._post_finish_depth == 0:
                    # Its own finally: if either call raised, a stale
                    # record would stay and look like a stuck section.
                    try:
                        self._dequeued_in_section.clear()
                        self._flush_loop_actions()
                    finally:
                        self._busy.stop()
    return wrapped
```

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for the busy sections of ScoringService and ProxyService (#38).

No database is needed: the scoring stops at its first SessionGen, the
rankings are mocked, and the services are built with their database
work patched out.

"""

import contextlib
import threading
import unittest
from datetime import datetime
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch

import requests

from cms.conf import Address
from cms.io.health import BusyTracker
from cms.io.priorityqueue import QueueEntry
from cms.service.ProxyService import ProxyExecutor, ProxyOperation, \
    ProxyService
from cms.service.ScoringService import ScoringExecutor, ScoringService
from cms.service.scoringoperations import ScoringOperation
from cmstestsuite.unit_tests.healthfakes import FakeClock, StopHere
from cmstestsuite.unit_tests.servicelogmixin import \
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin


RANKING = "http://rws:secret@localhost:8890/"
LOCAL = Address("127.0.0.1", 0)


def _entries(*operations: ProxyOperation) -> list[QueueEntry]:
    return [QueueEntry(operation, 0, datetime.now(), index)
            for index, operation in enumerate(operations)]


def _local_addresses() -> contextlib.ExitStack:
    """Patch the service addresses to a free loopback port."""
    stack = contextlib.ExitStack()
    stack.enter_context(patch("cms.io.async_service.get_service_address",
                              return_value=LOCAL))
    stack.enter_context(patch("cms.io.async_rpc.get_service_address",
                              return_value=LOCAL))
    return stack


class TestScoringSection(unittest.TestCase):

    def test_a_blocked_execution_is_the_section_score(self):
        tracker = BusyTracker()
        executor = ScoringExecutor(proxy_service=None, busy=tracker)
        entered, release = threading.Event(), threading.Event()
        self.addCleanup(release.set)
        errors: list[Exception] = []

        @contextlib.contextmanager
        def blocked_session():
            entered.set()
            release.wait(10)
            raise StopHere()
            yield  # Unreachable: it makes this a generator.

        def score():
            try:
                executor._execute_sync(ScoringOperation(1, 1))
            except StopHere as error:
                errors.append(error)

        with patch("cms.service.ScoringService.SessionGen",
                   blocked_session):
            thread = threading.Thread(target=score, daemon=True)
            thread.start()
            self.assertTrue(entered.wait(5))
            self.assertEqual([entry["what"] for entry in tracker.report()],
                             ["score"])
            release.set()
            thread.join(5)

        self.assertEqual(len(errors), 1)
        self.assertEqual(tracker.report(), [])

    def test_an_executor_built_without_a_tracker_has_its_own(self):
        self.assertIsInstance(
            ScoringExecutor(proxy_service=None)._busy, BusyTracker)


class TestServicesGiveTheirTracker(
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin, unittest.TestCase,
):
    """Built with no event loop running, so nothing starts."""

    def test_scoring_service(self):
        with _local_addresses(), patch.object(
                ScoringService, "start_sweeper",
                lambda self, timeout: None):
            service = ScoringService(0)
        self.assertIs(service.get_executor()._busy, service._busy)

    def test_proxy_service(self):
        with _local_addresses(), \
                patch.object(ProxyService, "start_sweeper",
                             lambda self, timeout: None), \
                patch.object(ProxyService, "_compute_group_contests",
                             lambda self, session: {}), \
                patch.object(ProxyService, "initialize",
                             lambda self: None):
            service = ProxyService(0)
        self.assertTrue(service._executors)
        for executor in service._executors:
            self.assertIs(executor._busy, service._busy)


class TestProxySection(unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        put_patcher = patch("cms.service.ProxyService.requests.put")
        self.requests_put = put_patcher.start()
        self.addCleanup(put_patcher.stop)
        self.requests_put.return_value.status_code = 200
        self.clock = FakeClock()
        self.tracker = BusyTracker(clock=self.clock)
        self.executor = ProxyExecutor(RANKING, busy=self.tracker)

    def test_a_blocked_send_is_the_section_send(self):
        entered, release = threading.Event(), threading.Event()
        self.addCleanup(release.set)

        def blocked_put(*args, **kwargs):
            entered.set()
            release.wait(10)
            return MagicMock(status_code=200)

        self.requests_put.side_effect = blocked_put
        batch = _entries(
            ProxyOperation(ProxyExecutor.USER_TYPE, {"u1": {}}, None))
        thread = threading.Thread(target=self.executor._execute_sync,
                                  args=(batch,), daemon=True)
        thread.start()
        self.assertTrue(entered.wait(5))

        self.assertEqual(
            [(entry["what"], entry["statements"])
             for entry in self.tracker.report()],
            [("send", 1)])
        release.set()
        thread.join(5)
        self.assertEqual(self.tracker.report(), [])

    def test_each_request_is_a_step(self):
        seen: list[tuple] = []

        def put(*args, **kwargs):
            entry = self.tracker.report()[0]
            seen.append(
                (entry["statements"], entry["idle_s"], entry["age_s"]))
            self.clock.now += 10
            return MagicMock(status_code=200)

        self.requests_put.side_effect = put
        unsent = self.executor._execute_sync(_entries(
            ProxyOperation(ProxyExecutor.CONTEST_TYPE, {"c1": {}}, None),
            ProxyOperation(ProxyExecutor.USER_TYPE, {"u1": {}}, None)))

        self.assertEqual(unsent, [])
        self.assertEqual(seen, [(1, 0, 0), (2, 0, 10)])
        self.assertEqual(self.tracker.report(), [])

    async def test_the_retry_wait_is_outside_the_section(self):
        self.requests_put.side_effect = \
            requests.exceptions.ConnectionError("down")
        during_wait: list[list[dict]] = []

        async def wait_for_retry(delay: float):
            during_wait.append(self.tracker.report())

        with patch.object(self.executor, "_wait_for_retry",
                          wait_for_retry):
            with self.assertLogs("cms.service.ProxyService",
                                 level="WARNING"):
                await self.executor.execute(_entries(ProxyOperation(
                    ProxyExecutor.USER_TYPE, {"u1": {}}, None)))

        self.assertEqual(during_wait, [[]])

    def test_an_executor_built_without_a_tracker_has_its_own(self):
        self.assertIsInstance(ProxyExecutor(RANKING)._busy, BusyTracker)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```

```python
class TestStatusOfDisabledWorkers(unittest.TestCase):
    """The fields cmsWatchdog (#38) reads from workers_status."""

    def setUp(self):
        self.pool = _single_worker_pool()
        self.coord = ServiceCoord("Worker", 0)

    def _time_out(self) -> int:
        shard = self.pool.acquire_worker([_operation("001")])
        self.pool._start_time[shard] = \
            make_datetime() - timedelta(seconds=601)
        with self.assertLogs("cms.service.workerpool", level="ERROR"):
            self.pool.check_timeouts()
        return shard

    def test_a_new_worker_is_not_disabled(self):
        status = self.pool.get_status()["0"]
        self.assertIs(status["disabled_by_timeout"], False)
        self.assertIsNone(status["disabled_at"])

    def test_the_timeout_sets_disabled_at(self):
        before = make_timestamp(make_datetime())
        self._time_out()
        after = make_timestamp(make_datetime())

        status = self.pool.get_status()["0"]

        self.assertIs(status["disabled_by_timeout"], True)
        self.assertGreaterEqual(status["disabled_at"], before)
        self.assertLessEqual(status["disabled_at"], after)
        # The RPC answer must stay JSON.
        json.dumps(self.pool.get_status())

    def test_reconnecting_clears_disabled_at(self):
        self._time_out()
        with self.assertLogs("cms.service.workerpool", level="WARNING"):
            self.pool.on_worker_connected(self.coord)
        status = self.pool.get_status()["0"]
        self.assertIs(status["disabled_by_timeout"], False)
        self.assertIsNone(status["disabled_at"])

    def test_enabling_or_disabling_by_hand_clears_disabled_at(self):
        shard = self._time_out()
        self.pool.enable_worker(shard)
        self.assertIsNone(self.pool.get_status()["0"]["disabled_at"])

        self._time_out()
        self.pool.enable_worker(shard)
        self.pool.disable_worker(shard)
        status = self.pool.get_status()["0"]
        self.assertIs(status["disabled_by_timeout"], False)
        self.assertIsNone(status["disabled_at"])
```

  - Create `cmstestsuite/unit_tests/db/pool_timeout_test.py`:

```python
        return: dict of info: current operation, starting time,
            number of errors, additional data specified in the

                                if disabled_at is not None else None)}
        return result
```

**Files:**

  (asyncio group)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from other tasks.
- Produces: `cms.io.web_service.log_request(handler) -> None`, the
  Tornado `log_function` of every WebService unless its `parameters`
  give another one. A successful (`< 400`) `GET /static/favicon.ico` is
  logged at DEBUG; every other request exactly as Tornado logs it today
  (same `tornado.access` logger, levels and `"%d %s %.2fms"` format,
  which `cmstestsuite/loadtest/faultstats.py` and `analyze.py` parse).
  Note: the spec's Testing section says "a 404 or any other path at
  INFO", but its Design (2.5, binding) says "every other request is
  logged as today", and Tornado logs a 404 at WARNING today; this plan
  follows 2.5.

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for the request log of WebService: probes at DEBUG (#38)."""

import asyncio
import logging
import re
import unittest
from unittest.mock import patch

import tornado.web

from cms.conf import Address
from cms.io.web_service import WebService
from cmstestsuite.unit_tests.servicelogmixin import \
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin


class HelloHandler(tornado.web.RequestHandler):
    def get(self):
        self.write("hello")


class TestRequestLog(
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin, unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase,
):

    @patch("cms.io.async_service.get_service_address",
           return_value=Address("127.0.0.1", 0))
    async def asyncSetUp(self, _address):
        self.custom_calls: list[str] = []
        self.service = WebService(
            listen_port=0, handlers=[(r"/", HelloHandler)],
            parameters={"static_files": [("cms.server", "static")]},
            shard=0, listen_address="127.0.0.1")
        self.run_task = asyncio.create_task(self.service._async_run())
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        self.addAsyncCleanup(self._stop)
        self.port = self.service._http_server_sockets[0].getsockname()[1]

    async def _stop(self):
        self.service.exit()
        await asyncio.wait_for(self.run_task, timeout=5)

    async def _request(self, method: str, path: str) -> logging.LogRecord:
        """Send one request; return the access log record it produced."""
        with self.assertLogs("tornado.access", level="DEBUG") as logs:
            reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection(
                "127.0.0.1", self.port)
            try:
                writer.write(("%s %s HTTP/1.0\r\n\r\n"
                              % (method, path)).encode())
                await writer.drain()
                await asyncio.wait_for(reader.read(), timeout=5)
            finally:
                writer.close()
            # The log line is written once the response is finished.
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        self.assertEqual(len(logs.records), 1)
        return logs.records[0]

    async def test_a_successful_favicon_probe_is_logged_at_debug(self):
        record = await self._request("GET", "/static/favicon.ico")
        self.assertEqual(record.levelno, logging.DEBUG)
        self.assertRegex(
            record.getMessage(),
            r"^200 GET /static/favicon\.ico \(127\.0\.0\.1\) "
            r"[0-9]+\.[0-9]{2}ms$")

    async def test_another_path_is_logged_at_info(self):
        record = await self._request("GET", "/")
        self.assertEqual(record.levelno, logging.INFO)
        self.assertTrue(re.match(r"^200 GET / ", record.getMessage()))

    async def test_a_favicon_head_is_logged_at_info(self):
        record = await self._request("HEAD", "/static/favicon.ico")
        self.assertEqual(record.levelno, logging.INFO)

    async def test_a_failed_favicon_is_logged_as_today(self):
        with patch("cms.io.static_handler.MultiLocationStaticFileHandler"
                   ".get", side_effect=tornado.web.HTTPError(404)):
            record = await self._request("GET", "/static/favicon.ico")
        self.assertEqual(record.levelno, logging.WARNING)
        self.assertTrue(record.getMessage().startswith("404 GET "))


class TestCustomLogFunction(
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin, unittest.TestCase,
):

    @patch("cms.io.async_service.get_service_address",
           return_value=Address("127.0.0.1", 0))
    def test_a_subclass_may_give_its_own(self, _address):
        def custom(handler):
            pass

        service = WebService(
            listen_port=0, handlers=[], parameters={"log_function": custom},
            shard=0, listen_address="127.0.0.1")
        self.assertIs(service.application.settings["log_function"], custom)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Edit `cms/io/web_service.py`.**
  - Imports: after `import tornado.web` (line 29), add
    `from tornado.log import access_log`.
  - Before `class StaticFileHasher:` (line 75), add:

```python
# The path that reverse proxies and cmsWatchdog (#38) probe every few
# seconds.
PROBE_PATH = "/static/favicon.ico"


def log_request(handler: tornado.web.RequestHandler) -> None:
    """Log a finished request like Tornado does, but probes at DEBUG.

    A successful GET of PROBE_PATH is logged at DEBUG, so that health
    checks do not flood the logs. Every other request is logged exactly
    as Tornado's Application.log_request does: the same logger, levels
    and format, which the load-test analysis parses.

    handler: the handler that served the request.

    """
    status = handler.get_status()
    request = handler.request
    if status < 400:
        if request.method == "GET" and request.path == PROBE_PATH:
            log_method = access_log.debug
        else:
            log_method = access_log.info
    elif status < 500:
        log_method = access_log.warning
    else:
        log_method = access_log.error
    log_method("%d %s %.2fms", status, handler._request_summary(),
               1000.0 * request.request_time())


```

  - Replace the `Application` call (lines 178-179):

```python
        self.application = tornado.web.Application(
            infra_handlers + handlers,
            **{"log_function": log_request, **parameters})
```

```python
def test_supervisord_every_program_stops_as_a_group_and_retries(
        monkeypatch):
    # stopasgroup and killasgroup: stopping a program also ends what it
    # started (a Worker's isolate keeper). startretries=30: supervisord's
    # backoff is linear, so a program that cannot start (the database is
    # down) goes FATAL after about 8 minutes, not 7 seconds (#38).
    _set(monkeypatch, {
        "CMS_CWS_COUNT": "2", "CMS_WORKER_COUNT": "2",
        "CMS_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN": "t", "CMS_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID": "c",
        "CMS_LOADER_SESSION_SECRET": "s", "CMS_LOADER_ADMIN_USER": "u",
        "CMS_LOADER_ADMIN_PASSWORD": "p"})
    programs = _supervisord_programs(gc.generate_supervisord_conf())
    assert {"cmstelegrambot", "cmsloader", "cmsworker1",
            "cmscontestwebserver1"} <= set(programs)
    for name, program in programs.items():
        assert program["stopasgroup"] == "true", name
        assert program["killasgroup"] == "true", name
        assert program["startretries"] == "30", name
        assert "startsecs" not in program, name


def test_cms_toml_has_no_checker(monkeypatch):
    # Nothing starts a Checker, so the Admin Web Server listed it as not
    # running (#38).
    _set(monkeypatch)
    services = tomllib.loads(gc.generate_cms_toml())["services"]
    assert "Checker" not in services
    assert "LogService" in services
```

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for status.sh, run with a fake docker on PATH."""

import os
import shutil
import subprocess
import textwrap

import pytest

REPO = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))

# It answers status.sh's commands from FAKE_* variables, and logs each
# command line to FAKE_LOG when it is set.
FAKE_DOCKER = textwrap.dedent("""\
    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    if [[ -n "${FAKE_LOG:-}" ]]; then echo "$*" >>"$FAKE_LOG"; fi
    case " $* " in
      *" supervisorctl "*)
        echo "cmslogservice   RUNNING   pid 10, uptime 1:00:00"
        exit "${FAKE_SUPERVISORCTL_RC:-0}" ;;
      *" cmsWatchdog "*)
        echo "${FAKE_WATCHDOG_LINE:-watchdog: observe}"
        exit "${FAKE_WATCHDOG_RC:-0}" ;;
      *" ps "*)
        echo "NAME   STATUS"
        exit 0 ;;
    esac
    exit 0
    """)


@pytest.fixture
def run_status(tmp_path):
    """Return a function that runs status.sh with the given FAKE_* env."""
    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
    (repo / "docker").mkdir(parents=True)
    shutil.copy(os.path.join(REPO, "status.sh"), repo / "status.sh")
    shutil.copy(os.path.join(REPO, "docker", "_lib.sh"),
                repo / "docker" / "_lib.sh")
    bin_dir = tmp_path / "bin"
    bin_dir.mkdir()
    docker = bin_dir / "docker"
    docker.write_text(FAKE_DOCKER)
    docker.chmod(0o755)

    def run(**env: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess:
        environment = {"PATH": "%s:%s" % (bin_dir, os.environ["PATH"]),
                       "HOME": str(tmp_path), **env}
        return subprocess.run(["bash", str(repo / "status.sh")],
                              env=environment, capture_output=True,
                              text=True, timeout=30)

    return run


@pytest.mark.parametrize("code", ["0", "3"])
def test_the_table_is_shown_when_supervisorctl_answers(run_status, code):
    # 3: some program is not RUNNING, a STOPPED one included.
    result = run_status(FAKE_SUPERVISORCTL_RC=code)
    assert result.returncode == 0
    assert "cmslogservice" in result.stdout
    assert "(container not running)" not in result.stdout
    assert "did not answer" not in result.stdout


def test_a_supervisord_that_does_not_answer_is_named(run_status):
    result = run_status(FAKE_SUPERVISORCTL_RC="124")
    assert result.returncode == 0
    assert "(supervisord did not answer in 10 s)" in result.stdout
    assert "(container not running)" not in result.stdout


@pytest.mark.parametrize("code", ["1", "125"])
def test_other_failures_mean_the_container_is_not_running(
        run_status, code):
    result = run_status(FAKE_SUPERVISORCTL_RC=code)
    assert result.returncode == 0
    assert "(container not running)" in result.stdout


def test_supervisorctl_runs_under_a_10_s_timeout(run_status, tmp_path):
    log = tmp_path / "docker.log"
    run_status(FAKE_LOG=str(log))
    commands = log.read_text().splitlines()
    # With spaces: pytest's tmp_path holds the test's name.
    status = [line for line in commands if " supervisorctl " in line]
    assert len(status) == 1
    assert "exec -T cms timeout 10 supervisorctl -c " in status[0]
    assert status[0].endswith(" status")
```

```bash
  124) echo "(supervisord did not answer in 10 s)" ;;
```

    `hhmm_utc(wall: float) -> str`,

    `is_number(value: object) -> bool`;

    application_name=None, client_port=None, state=None,

    xact_age_s=None, query_age_s=None, state_age_s=None, blocked_by=(),

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for the data model and the ledger of cmsWatchdog's policy."""

import unittest

from cms.watchdog.policy import ENFORCE, OBSERVE, RESTART, START, \
    SERVICES_POOL, WORKERS_POOL, Booking, Ledger, LedgerError, \
    PolicyState, ProgramFacts, age, hhmm_utc, is_number, p99, pool_of


WALL = 1_800_000_000.0


def booking(program: str, wall: float, pool: str = SERVICES_POOL,
            action: str = RESTART, would: bool = False,
            label: str = "") -> Booking:
    return Booking(wall, program, label or program, pool, action, "R1",
                   would, 4242 if action == RESTART else None)


class TestHelpers(unittest.TestCase):

    def test_program_facts(self):
        cws = ProgramFacts("cmscontestwebserver2", "ContestWebServer", 2,
                           "RUNNING", 10, WALL)
        self.assertTrue(cws.managed)
        self.assertEqual(cws.klass, "web")
        self.assertEqual(cws.label, "cmsContestWebServer 2")
        for kind, shard in (("TelegramBot", 0), (None, None),
                            ("Worker", None), ("Watchdog", 0)):
            self.assertFalse(
                ProgramFacts("x", kind, shard, "RUNNING", 1, WALL).managed)

    def test_pools(self):
        self.assertEqual(pool_of("workers"), WORKERS_POOL)
        for klass in ("web", "core", "infra"):
            self.assertEqual(pool_of(klass), SERVICES_POOL)

    def test_a_negative_age_is_zero(self):
        self.assertEqual(age(100.0, 160.0), 0.0)
        self.assertEqual(age(160.0, 100.0), 60.0)

    def test_hhmm_utc(self):
        self.assertEqual(hhmm_utc(WALL), "08:00 UTC")
        self.assertEqual(hhmm_utc(WALL + 3000), "08:50 UTC")

    def test_p99(self):
        self.assertIsNone(p99([]))
        self.assertEqual(p99([0.5]), 0.5)
        self.assertEqual(p99([float(i) for i in range(1, 101)]), 99.0)

    def test_is_number(self):
        for value in (0, 1.5, -3):
            self.assertTrue(is_number(value))
        for value in (True, None, "1", float("nan"), float("inf")):
            self.assertFalse(is_number(value))

    def test_shadow_for(self):
        state = PolicyState(mode=ENFORCE,
                            restart_classes=frozenset({"web"}))
        self.assertFalse(state.shadow_for("web"))
        self.assertTrue(state.shadow_for("workers"))
        observe = PolicyState(mode=OBSERVE, restart_classes=frozenset())
        self.assertFalse(observe.shadow_for("workers"))


class TestBudgets(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.ledger = Ledger()

    def free_at(self, program: str, pool: str = SERVICES_POOL,
                action: str = RESTART, shadow: bool = False,
                wall: float = WALL) -> float | None:
        return self.ledger.free_at(program, pool, action, shadow, wall)

    def test_two_restarts_per_program(self):
        self.ledger.book(booking("cws1", WALL - 600), False)
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("cws1"))
        self.ledger.book(booking("cws1", WALL - 300), False)
        self.assertEqual(self.free_at("cws1"), WALL - 600 + 3600)
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("cws0"))

    def test_three_restarts_per_pool(self):
        for index, program in enumerate(("cws0", "es", "ss")):
            self.ledger.book(booking(program, WALL - 900 + index), False)
        self.assertEqual(self.free_at("aws"), WALL - 900 + 3600)

    def test_the_workers_have_a_pool_of_their_own(self):
        for program in ("cws0", "es", "ss"):
            self.ledger.book(booking(program, WALL - 60), False)
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("worker0", WORKERS_POOL))
        for program in ("worker0", "worker1", "worker2"):
            self.ledger.book(booking(program, WALL - 30, WORKERS_POOL),
                             False)
        self.assertEqual(self.free_at("worker3", WORKERS_POOL),
                         WALL - 30 + 3600)
        self.ledger.real = [b for b in self.ledger.real
                            if b.pool == WORKERS_POOL]
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("aws"))

    def test_two_restarts_per_worker_inside_the_workers_pool(self):
        self.ledger.book(booking("worker0", WALL - 600, WORKERS_POOL),
                         False)
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("worker0", WORKERS_POOL))
        self.ledger.book(booking("worker0", WALL - 300, WORKERS_POOL),
                         False)
        # 2 of the pool's 3: the other Workers may still restart.
        self.assertEqual(self.free_at("worker0", WORKERS_POOL),
                         WALL - 600 + 3600)
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("worker1", WORKERS_POOL))

    def test_fatal_starts_have_their_own_budget(self):
        self.ledger.book(booking("es", WALL - 100, action=START), False)
        self.ledger.book(booking("es", WALL - 50, action=START), False)
        self.assertEqual(self.free_at("es", action=START),
                         WALL - 100 + 3600)
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("es"))
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("ss", action=START))

    def test_the_shadow_ledger_is_separate(self):
        self.ledger.book(booking("worker0", WALL - 10, WORKERS_POOL,
                                 would=True), True)
        self.ledger.book(booking("worker0", WALL - 5, WORKERS_POOL,
                                 would=True), True)
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("worker0", WORKERS_POOL))
        self.assertEqual(
            self.free_at("worker0", WORKERS_POOL, shadow=True),
            WALL - 10 + 3600)
        self.assertEqual(self.ledger.count("worker0", RESTART, True, WALL),
                         2)
        self.assertEqual(self.ledger.count("worker0", RESTART, False, WALL),
                         0)

    def test_the_window_slides(self):
        self.ledger.book(booking("cws1", WALL - 3600), False)
        self.ledger.book(booking("cws1", WALL - 100), False)
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("cws1"))
        self.assertEqual(self.ledger.count("cws1", RESTART, False, WALL), 1)

    def test_a_booking_in_the_future_counts(self):
        # The wall clock stepped back: its age is 0, not negative.
        self.ledger.book(booking("cws1", WALL + 500), False)
        self.ledger.book(booking("cws1", WALL + 600), False)
        self.assertEqual(self.free_at("cws1"), WALL + 500 + 3600)

    def test_an_exhausted_ledger_blocks_everything_for_an_hour(self):
        self.ledger = Ledger.exhausted(WALL)
        for action in (RESTART, START):
            for shadow in (False, True):
                self.assertEqual(
                    self.free_at("cws1", action=action, shadow=shadow),
                    WALL + 3600)
        self.ledger.prune(WALL + 3600)
        self.assertIsNone(self.free_at("cws1", wall=WALL + 3600))

    def test_prune_keeps_what_the_last_hour_needs(self):
        self.ledger.book(booking("cws1", WALL - 4000), False)
        self.ledger.book(booking("cws1", WALL - 10), False)
        self.ledger.red = [[WALL - 9000, WALL - 4000],
                           [WALL - 300, WALL - 200], [WALL - 50, None]]
        self.ledger.prune(WALL)
        self.assertEqual([b.wall for b in self.ledger.real], [WALL - 10])
        self.assertEqual(self.ledger.red,
                         [[WALL - 300, WALL - 200], [WALL - 50, None]])


class TestRedIntervals(unittest.TestCase):

    def test_red_since(self):
        ledger = Ledger(red=[[WALL - 300, WALL - 200]])
        self.assertTrue(ledger.red_since(WALL - 250))
        self.assertFalse(ledger.red_since(WALL - 200))
        ledger.red.append([WALL - 10, None])
        self.assertTrue(ledger.red_since(WALL + 1000))

    def test_last_restart_of(self):
        ledger = Ledger()
        self.assertIsNone(ledger.last_restart_of("cmsEvaluationService 0"))
        ledger.book(booking("es", WALL - 50, would=True,
                            label="cmsEvaluationService 0"), False)
        self.assertIsNone(ledger.last_restart_of("cmsEvaluationService 0"))
        ledger.book(booking("es", WALL - 20,
                            label="cmsEvaluationService 0"), False)
        self.assertEqual(
            ledger.last_restart_of("cmsEvaluationService 0").wall,
            WALL - 20)


class TestLedgerFile(unittest.TestCase):

    def test_a_round_trip(self):
        ledger = Ledger()
        ledger.book(booking("cws1", WALL - 10), False)
        ledger.book(booking("worker0", WALL - 5, WORKERS_POOL,
                            would=True), True)
        ledger.red = [[WALL - 300, WALL - 200], [WALL - 50, None]]
        ledger.incidents = {"cws1": {"rule": "R1", "opened": WALL - 90,
                                     "passes": 1}}
        ledger.exhausted_until = WALL + 100
        self.assertEqual(Ledger.from_json(ledger.to_json()), ledger)

    def test_what_is_not_a_ledger(self):
        for text in ("", "{", "[]", "{}", '{"real": 3}',
                     '{"real": [{"wall": "x"}], "shadow": [], "red": [],'
                     ' "incidents": {}, "exhausted_until": null}',
                     '{"real": [], "shadow": [], "red": [[1]],'
                     ' "incidents": {}, "exhausted_until": null}'):
            with self.subTest(text=text):
                with self.assertRaises(LedgerError):
                    Ledger.from_json(text)
        self.assertTrue(issubclass(LedgerError, ValueError))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```
