```python


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Target:
    """The process an action is about: a program and its pid."""

    name: str
    kind: str
    pid: int


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Outcome:
    """What an action did.

    result: "restarted"; "unconfirmed" (signalled, but no new pid was
        RUNNING in time); "would" (a dry run); "started" or "start
        failed" (a batch); "evidence" (a snapshot); "refused" (the pid
        changed or it is not RUNNING, nothing was signalled); "gone"
        (it had exited); "unbooked" (the ledger could not be written,
        nothing was done).
    detail: a sentence for the alert.
    new_pid: the program's pid after a restart.
    killed: whether some process of the group was left for KILL after
        the TERM wait.
    usr1: what happened to the evidence USR1.
    evidence_error: why the evidence could not be written, if it
        could not.

    """

    result: str
    detail: str = ""
    new_pid: int | None = None
    killed: bool = False
    usr1: str = ""
    evidence_error: str = ""


def handles_signal(pid: int, signum: int, proc: str = "/proc") -> bool:
    """Tell whether a process catches signum (SigCgt of its status)."""
    try:
        with open("%s/%d/status" % (proc, pid)) as f:
            for line in f:
                if line.startswith("SigCgt:"):
                    return bool(int(line.split()[1], 16)
                                & (1 << (signum - 1)))
    except (OSError, ValueError, IndexError):
        return False
    return False


def process_state(pid: int, proc: str = "/proc") -> str | None:
    """Return a process's state letter, or None if it is gone."""
    try:
        return stat_fields("%s/%d/stat" % (proc, pid))[0]
    except (OSError, ValueError, IndexError):
        return None


def group_members(pgid: int, proc: str = "/proc") -> list[int]:
    """Return the processes of a process group that are not zombies."""
    members = []
    for entry in os.listdir(proc):
        if not entry.isdecimal():
            continue
        try:
            fields = stat_fields("%s/%s/stat" % (proc, entry))
        except (OSError, ValueError, IndexError):
            continue
        if len(fields) > 2 and fields[2] == str(pgid) \
                and fields[0] not in ("Z", "X"):
            members.append(int(entry))
    return members


async def wait_readable(fd: int, timeout: float) -> bool:
    """Wait until fd is readable: for a pidfd, until the process exits.

    fd: the file descriptor.
    timeout: how long to wait at most, in seconds.

    return: whether it became readable in time.

    """
    loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
    ready = loop.create_future()

    def on_ready() -> None:
        if not ready.done():
            ready.set_result(None)

    loop.add_reader(fd, on_ready)
    try:
        await asyncio.wait_for(ready, timeout)
        return True
    except TimeoutError:
        return False
    finally:
        loop.remove_reader(fd)


class DryRunActuator:
    """The actuator of would-actions (3.7).

    It books, checks that the pid is still the program's, writes the
    evidence and sends the evidence USR1 (only to a process that
    handles it), and then does nothing else: main.py logs "WOULD ...".

    """

    def __init__(self, source: ProcessSource, *, proc: str = "/proc",
                 usr1_wait_s: float = 1.0,
                 pidfd_open: Callable[[int], int] = os.pidfd_open,
                 send_signal: Callable[[int, int], None] =
                 signal.pidfd_send_signal):
        """Prepare the actuator.

        source: the process source (programs(), and start() for
            SignalActuator).
        proc: where /proc is.
        usr1_wait_s: how long faulthandler gets to write the stacks.
        pidfd_open, send_signal: os.pidfd_open and
            signal.pidfd_send_signal (tests may replace them).

        """
        self._source = source
        self._proc = proc
        self._usr1_wait_s = usr1_wait_s
        self._pidfd_open = pidfd_open
        self._send_signal = send_signal

    @staticmethod
    def _book(book: Callable[[], None]) -> Outcome | None:
        """Book an action, its first step (3.7, step 0).

        book: writes the ledger and the journal.

        return: None once booked, or the "unbooked" outcome when the
            ledger could not be written: the action stops there.

        """
        try:
            book()
        except OSError as error:
            return Outcome("unbooked", "its ledger could not be written: "
                           "%s" % error)
        return None

    async def _lookup(self, name: str) -> ProcessInfo | None:
        for info in await self._source.programs():
            if info.name == name:
                return info
        return None

    async def _open_verified(self,
                             target: Target) -> tuple[int, Outcome | None]:
        """Open a pidfd of target, still the program's (3.7, step 1).

        Opening it first, then re-reading the source, binds the pidfd to
        the process the source names: a pid reused later cannot be hit.

        return: (pidfd, None), or (-1, the refusal).

        """
        try:
            pidfd = self._pidfd_open(target.pid)
        except ProcessLookupError:
            return -1, Outcome("gone", "pid %d had already exited"
                               % target.pid)
        try:
            current = await self._lookup(target.name)
        except Exception as error:
            os.close(pidfd)
            return -1, Outcome("refused", "the process source did not "
                               "answer: %s" % error)
        if current is None or current.state != "RUNNING" \
                or current.pid != target.pid:
            os.close(pidfd)
            return -1, Outcome("refused", "it is no longer RUNNING as pid "
                               "%d" % target.pid)
        return pidfd, None

    async def _evidence(self, pidfd: int, target: Target,
                        write_evidence: Callable[[], None]
                        ) -> tuple[str, str]:
        """Write the evidence, then send the USR1 if it is safe (3.8).

        return: (what happened to the USR1, the evidence's error or "").

        """
        evidence_error = ""
        try:
            write_evidence()
        except Exception as error:
            evidence_error = " ".join(str(error).split()) \
                or type(error).__name__
        if target.kind not in USR1_KINDS:
            return ("no USR1: %s is not a CMS service" % target.kind,
                    evidence_error)
        if process_state(target.pid, self._proc) == "T":
            return ("no USR1: the process is stopped (state T)",
                    evidence_error)
        if not handles_signal(target.pid, signal.SIGUSR1, self._proc):
            return "no USR1: the process does not handle it", evidence_error
        try:
            # The leader only, never the group: a Worker's isolate keeper
            # does not handle USR1, and it would die of it.
            self._send_signal(pidfd, signal.SIGUSR1)
        except ProcessLookupError:
            return "no USR1: the process exited", evidence_error
        await asyncio.sleep(self._usr1_wait_s)
        return "USR1 sent: its stacks are in ./logs.sh", evidence_error

    async def snapshot(self, target: Target,
                       write_evidence: Callable[[], None]) -> Outcome:
        """Write the evidence and send the USR1, booking nothing.

        For cmsWatchdog --snapshot, and steps 1 and 2 of an action.

        """
        pidfd, refusal = await self._open_verified(target)
        if refusal is not None:
            return refusal
        try:
            usr1, evidence_error = await self._evidence(pidfd, target,
                                                        write_evidence)
        finally:
            os.close(pidfd)
        return Outcome("evidence", usr1=usr1, evidence_error=evidence_error)

    async def restart(self, target: Target, book: Callable[[], None],
                      write_evidence: Callable[[], None]) -> Outcome:
        """Book a would-restart, then do steps 1 and 2 only."""
        unbooked = self._book(book)
        if unbooked is not None:
            return unbooked
        outcome = await self.snapshot(target, write_evidence)
        if outcome.result != "evidence":
            return outcome
        return Outcome("would", usr1=outcome.usr1,
                       evidence_error=outcome.evidence_error)

    async def start(self, names: Sequence[str],
                    book: Callable[[], None]) -> Outcome:
        """Book a would-start of a batch; start nothing."""
        unbooked = self._book(book)
        if unbooked is not None:
            return unbooked
        return Outcome("would")


class SignalActuator(DryRunActuator):
    """The actuator of enforce mode (3.7).

    A restart is a signal to the program's process group: TERM, a wait
    for the leader to exit, KILL to whatever is left, and supervisord's
    autorestart starts it again. It never runs supervisorctl.

    """

    def __init__(self, source: SupervisorSource, *,
                 term_wait_s: float = 10.0, confirm_s: float = 30.0,
                 poll_s: float = 1.0,
                 killpg: Callable[[int, int], None] = os.killpg,
                 **kwargs: Any):
        """Prepare the actuator.

        source: a process source that can act (SupervisorSource).
        term_wait_s: how long TERM gets before KILL.
        confirm_s: how long the new pid gets to be RUNNING.
        poll_s: how often the source is read meanwhile.
        killpg: os.killpg (tests may wrap it).
        kwargs: those of DryRunActuator.

        """
        super().__init__(source, **kwargs)
        self._term_wait_s = term_wait_s
        self._confirm_s = confirm_s
        self._poll_s = poll_s
        self._killpg = killpg

    async def restart(self, target: Target, book: Callable[[], None],
                      write_evidence: Callable[[], None]) -> Outcome:
        """Restart target (3.7, steps 0 to 7).

        target: the program and the pid the policy saw.
        book: writes the ledger and the journal; an OSError stops the
            action before anything else.
        write_evidence: writes the evidence file; its failure is
            reported, and the action goes on.

        return: the outcome.

        """
        unbooked = self._book(book)
        if unbooked is not None:
            return unbooked
        pidfd, refusal = await self._open_verified(target)
        if refusal is not None:
            return refusal
        try:
            usr1, evidence_error = await self._evidence(pidfd, target,
                                                        write_evidence)
            try:
                self._killpg(target.pid, signal.SIGTERM)
            except ProcessLookupError:
                pass
            await wait_readable(pidfd, self._term_wait_s)
            killed = False
            if group_members(target.pid, self._proc):
                try:
                    self._killpg(target.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
                    killed = True
                except ProcessLookupError:
                    pass
        finally:
            os.close(pidfd)
        new_pid = await self._confirm(target)
        if new_pid is None:
            return Outcome("unconfirmed", "no new pid was RUNNING within "
                           "%g s" % self._confirm_s, killed=killed,
                           usr1=usr1, evidence_error=evidence_error)
        return Outcome("restarted", new_pid=new_pid, killed=killed,
                       usr1=usr1, evidence_error=evidence_error)

    async def _confirm(self, target: Target) -> int | None:
        """Return the new pid once RUNNING, or None after confirm_s."""
        loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
        deadline = loop.time() + self._confirm_s
        while True:
            try:
                current = await self._lookup(target.name)
            except Exception:
                current = None
            if current is not None and current.state == "RUNNING" \
                    and current.pid not in (None, target.pid):
                return current.pid
            if loop.time() >= deadline:
                return None
            await asyncio.sleep(self._poll_s)

    async def start(self, names: Sequence[str],
                    book: Callable[[], None]) -> Outcome:
        """Start a batch of FATAL programs, in order (R5)."""
        unbooked = self._book(book)
        if unbooked is not None:
            return unbooked
        failures = []
        for name in names:
            try:
                await self._source.start(name)
            except Exception as error:
                failures.append("%s: %s" % (name, error))
        if failures:
            return Outcome("start failed", "; ".join(failures))
        return Outcome("started")
```

### Task 16: The state files, the alert channels and the `--status` line

**Files:**
- Create: `cms/watchdog/main.py`
- Create: `cmstestsuite/unit_tests/watchdog/main_files_test.py`
  (asyncio group)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `policy.Ledger`, `LedgerError`, `Decision`, `hhmm_utc`,
  `CYCLE_S` (Tasks 8-11); `probes.RpcProbe`, `ProbeResult` (Task 12).
- Produces (in `cms/watchdog/main.py`), used by Task 17:
  - `utc(wall: float) -> str` (`"2027-01-15 08:00:00 UTC"`),
    `write_atomic(path: str, text: str) -> None`;
  - `class Files(run_dir: str, log_dir: str)` with the attributes
    `ledger`, `status`, `evidence_dir`, `journal` (paths) and
    `load_ledger(wall) -> tuple[policy.Ledger, str]` (the alert line,
    `""` when fine), `save_ledger(ledger)`, `save_status(status)`,
    `append_journal(record)`, `evidence_name(wall, program) -> str`,
    `write_evidence(name, evidence)`; the save and write methods raise
    `OSError`, and so does `save_ledger` for a ledger that `to_json`
    refuses (a NaN or infinite time: its `ValueError` becomes a failed
    write, which the callers already handle);
  - `load_ledger` treats a ledger that is not UTF-8 like one that does
    not parse (every budget exhausted for `CORRUPT_LEDGER_S`), and
    clamps what it read: `exhausted_until` to at most now +
    `CORRUPT_LEDGER_S`, and every booking's wall to at most now, so that
    a hand edit with a future time cannot freeze the budgets (spec
    3.10's one hour);
  - `log_record(level: str, text: str, wall: float) -> dict` (the
    keyword arguments of `LogService.Log`);
  - `class Alerter(log_service: probes.RpcProbe | None, out: TextIO |
    None = None)` with `emit(level, text, wall)`, `resend(alerts,
    wall)`, `async flush()`; `log_service` is an object with
    `async call(method, data) -> ProbeResult` (an `RpcProbe`, or a test
    fake), or None;
  - `status_line(path: str, environ: Mapping[str, str], now: float) ->
    str`.

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for cmsWatchdog's files, alert channels and --status line."""

import io
import json
import os
import shutil
import tempfile
import unittest

from cms.service.LogService import LogService
from cms.watchdog import policy
from cms.watchdog.main import Alerter, Files, log_record, status_line
from cms.watchdog.probes import ProbeResult
from cmstestsuite.unit_tests.servicelogmixin import \
    ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin


WALL = 1_800_000_000.0


class FakeLog:
    """Stands in for the RpcProbe to LogService: records each call."""

    def __init__(self, ok: bool = True):
        self.ok = ok
        self.calls: list[tuple[str, dict]] = []

    async def call(self, method: str, data: dict | None = None):
        self.calls.append((method, data))
        return ProbeResult(self.ok, 0.01 if self.ok else None,
                           "" if self.ok else "timed out")


class TestFiles(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.root = tempfile.mkdtemp()
        self.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, self.root)
        self.run_dir = os.path.join(self.root, "run")
        self.files = Files(self.run_dir, os.path.join(self.root, "log"))

    def test_a_missing_ledger_starts_empty(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.files.load_ledger(WALL), (policy.Ledger(), ""))

    def test_the_ledger_survives_a_restart(self):
        ledger = policy.Ledger()
        ledger.book(policy.Booking(
            WALL, "cmscontestwebserver1", "cmsContestWebServer 1",
            policy.SERVICES_POOL, policy.RESTART, "R1", False, 109), False)
        self.files.save_ledger(ledger)
        again = Files(self.run_dir, os.path.join(self.root, "log"))
        self.assertEqual(again.load_ledger(WALL + 60), (ledger, ""))
        self.assertEqual(os.listdir(self.run_dir), ["watchdog-ledger.json"])

    def test_a_ledger_that_does_not_parse_exhausts_every_budget(self):
        os.makedirs(self.run_dir)
        for content in (b"{broken", b'{"real": "\xff\xfe"}'):
            with self.subTest(content=content):
                with open(self.files.ledger, "wb") as f:
                    f.write(content)
                ledger, problem = self.files.load_ledger(WALL)
                self.assertEqual(ledger.exhausted_until, WALL + 3600)
                self.assertIn("did not parse", problem)
                self.assertIn("until %s" % policy.hhmm_utc(WALL + 3600),
                              problem)

    def test_a_future_ledger_is_brought_back_to_now(self):
        # A hand edit with times far ahead must not freeze the budgets
        # for years: at most an hour, as for a ledger that does not
        # parse (spec 3.10).
        ledger = policy.Ledger(exhausted_until=WALL + 10 * 365 * 86400)
        ledger.book(policy.Booking(
            WALL + 86400, "cmscontestwebserver1", "cmsContestWebServer 1",
            policy.SERVICES_POOL, policy.RESTART, "R1", False, 109), False)
        self.files.save_ledger(ledger)
        loaded, problem = self.files.load_ledger(WALL)
        self.assertEqual(problem, "")
        self.assertEqual(loaded.exhausted_until,
                         WALL + policy.CORRUPT_LEDGER_S)
        self.assertEqual([booking.wall for booking in loaded.real], [WALL])

    def test_a_ledger_json_cannot_hold_is_a_failed_write(self):
        ledger = policy.Ledger()
        ledger.book(policy.Booking(
            float("nan"), "cmscontestwebserver1", "cmsContestWebServer 1",
            policy.SERVICES_POOL, policy.RESTART, "R1", False, 109), False)
        with self.assertRaises(OSError):
            self.files.save_ledger(ledger)
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(self.files.ledger))

    def test_the_status_is_written_atomically(self):
        self.files.save_status({"time": WALL, "mode": "observe"})
        with open(self.files.status) as f:
            self.assertEqual(json.load(f)["mode"], "observe")
        self.assertEqual(os.listdir(self.run_dir), ["watchdog.json"])

    def test_the_journal_appends_lines(self):
        self.files.append_journal({"a": 1})
        self.files.append_journal({"b": 2})
        with open(self.files.journal) as f:
            self.assertEqual([json.loads(line) for line in f],
                             [{"a": 1}, {"b": 2}])

    def test_evidence(self):
        self.assertEqual(
            self.files.evidence_name(WALL, "cmscontestwebserver1"),
            "20270115T080000Z-cmscontestwebserver1.json")
        self.assertEqual(self.files.evidence_name(WALL, "cmsLogService 0"),
                         "20270115T080000Z-cmsLogService_0.json")
        self.files.write_evidence("x.json", {"rule": "R1"})
        with open(os.path.join(self.root, "log", "watchdog",
                               "x.json")) as f:
            self.assertEqual(json.load(f), {"rule": "R1"})


class TestAlerter(unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase):

    async def test_stdout_gets_everything_and_logservice_warnings(self):
        out, log = io.StringIO(), FakeLog()
        alerter = Alerter(log, out)
        alerter.emit("INFO", "Watchdog: summary", WALL)
        alerter.emit("WARNING", "Watchdog: held", WALL)
        await alerter.flush()
        self.assertEqual(out.getvalue().splitlines(), [
            "2027-01-15 08:00:00 UTC INFO Watchdog: summary",
            "2027-01-15 08:00:00 UTC WARNING Watchdog: held"])
        self.assertEqual(log.calls, [
            ("Log", log_record("WARNING", "Watchdog: held", WALL))])
        await alerter.flush()
        self.assertEqual(len(log.calls), 1)

    async def test_a_logservice_that_does_not_answer_costs_one_call(self):
        log = FakeLog(ok=False)
        alerter = Alerter(log, io.StringIO())
        for index in range(3):
            alerter.emit("ERROR", "Watchdog: %d" % index, WALL)
        await alerter.flush()
        self.assertEqual(len(log.calls), 1)

    async def test_open_alerts_are_sent_again(self):
        log = FakeLog()
        alerter = Alerter(log, io.StringIO())
        alerter.resend([
            policy.Decision(policy.ALERT, "Watchdog: red", "ERROR"),
            policy.Decision(policy.ALERT, "Watchdog: hourly", "INFO")],
            WALL)
        await alerter.flush()
        self.assertEqual([data["msg"] for _, data in log.calls],
                         ["Watchdog: red"])

    async def test_without_logservice(self):
        out = io.StringIO()
        alerter = Alerter(None, out)
        alerter.emit("WARNING", "Watchdog: held", WALL)
        alerter.resend([policy.Decision(policy.ALERT, "x")], WALL)
        await alerter.flush()
        self.assertIn("Watchdog: held", out.getvalue())


class TestLogRecord(ServiceLoggingIsolationMixin, unittest.TestCase):

    def test_logservice_rebuilds_it_for_the_overview(self):
        service = LogService(0)
        service.Log(**log_record(
            "WARNING", "Watchdog: restarted cmsContestWebServer 1", WALL))
        self.assertEqual(service.last_messages()[-1], {
            "message": "Watchdog: restarted cmsContestWebServer 1",
            "coord": "Watchdog,0", "operation": "", "severity": "WARNING",
            "timestamp": WALL, "exc_text": None})


class TestStatusLine(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        directory = tempfile.mkdtemp()
        self.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, directory)
        self.path = os.path.join(directory, "watchdog.json")

    def write(self, text: str) -> None:
        with open(self.path, "w") as f:
            f.write(text)

    def test_off(self):
        self.write(json.dumps({"time": WALL, "mode": "observe",
                               "open_alerts": []}))
        self.assertEqual(status_line(self.path, {"CMS_WATCHDOG": "Off"},
                                     WALL),
                         "watchdog: off (not configured)")

    def test_no_status_file(self):
        self.assertEqual(status_line(self.path, {}, WALL), "no status file")

    def test_a_status(self):
        self.write(json.dumps({"time": WALL - 4, "mode": "observe",
                               "open_alerts": [], "supervisord": "ok"}))
        self.assertEqual(status_line(self.path, {}, WALL),
                         "watchdog: observe, last cycle 4 s ago, "
                         "0 open alerts")

    def test_stale_and_unreachable(self):
        self.write(json.dumps({"time": WALL - 300, "mode": "enforce",
                               "open_alerts": ["a", "b"],
                               "supervisord": "unreachable"}))
        self.assertEqual(
            status_line(self.path, {}, WALL),
            "watchdog: enforce, last cycle 300 s ago, 2 open alerts, "
            "supervisord unreachable (stale: is cmswatchdog running? "
            "See ./status.sh)")

    def test_a_status_that_does_not_parse(self):
        self.write("{")
        self.assertTrue(status_line(self.path, {}, WALL).startswith(
            "watchdog: the status file"))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```

```python
# (fork header)

"""cmsWatchdog: restart a CMS program that is alive but hung (#38).

    cmsWatchdog --mode observe|enforce --restart web,core,infra
        [--process-source supervisor|proc] [--status-history PATH]
    cmsWatchdog --status
    cmsWatchdog --snapshot PROGRAM

It runs as the supervisord program cmswatchdog (see
docker/generate_config.py). Every 15 seconds it probes the programs,
asks policy.decide() what to do, acts through an actuator, and writes
its status, its ledger and its journal. "When the watchdog alerts" in
docs/contest-day.md says what its alerts mean; the design is in
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-watchdog-design.md.

"""

import json
import os
import sys
import time
from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
from typing import TextIO

from cms.watchdog import policy, probes


LEDGER_NAME = "watchdog-ledger.json"
STATUS_NAME = "watchdog.json"
EVIDENCE_DIR = "watchdog"
JOURNAL_NAME = "actions.jsonl"
LEVELS = {"INFO": 20, "WARNING": 30, "ERROR": 40}


def utc(wall: float) -> str:
    """Return an epoch time as "2027-01-15 08:00:00 UTC"."""
    return time.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S UTC", time.gmtime(wall))


def write_atomic(path: str, text: str) -> None:
    """Write a file through a temporary file and a rename.

    raise (OSError): if it cannot be written.

    """
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path), exist_ok=True)
    partial = path + ".tmp"
    with open(partial, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(text)
    os.replace(partial, path)


class Files:
    """Where the watchdog keeps its state, and how it writes it (3.10).

    The ledger and the status are in the run directory (a tmpfs in
    production: they survive a restart of the watchdog, not one of the
    container); the journal and the evidence are in log/watchdog/ (the
    cms-logs volume).

    """

    def __init__(self, run_dir: str, log_dir: str):
        """Name the files.

        run_dir: config.global_.run_dir.
        log_dir: config.global_.log_dir.

        """
        self.ledger = os.path.join(run_dir, LEDGER_NAME)
        self.status = os.path.join(run_dir, STATUS_NAME)
        self.evidence_dir = os.path.join(log_dir, EVIDENCE_DIR)
        self.journal = os.path.join(self.evidence_dir, JOURNAL_NAME)

    def load_ledger(self, wall: float) -> tuple[policy.Ledger, str]:
        """Read the ledger at startup.

        A missing ledger starts empty. One that exists but cannot be
        read (it does not parse, or is not UTF-8) counts as every budget
        exhausted for an hour, so that a crash that corrupts it cannot
        reset the budgets. Times ahead of now are brought back: at most
        an hour of exhausted budgets, no booking in the future.

        wall: now (epoch seconds).

        return: the ledger, and an alert line ("" if it was fine).

        """
        try:
            with open(self.ledger, encoding="utf-8") as f:
                ledger = policy.Ledger.from_json(f.read())
        except FileNotFoundError:
            return policy.Ledger(), ""
        except (OSError, ValueError) as error:
            # ValueError: a LedgerError, or a file that is not UTF-8.
            ledger = policy.Ledger.exhausted(wall)
            return ledger, (
                "Watchdog: its ledger %s did not parse (%s): every restart "
                "budget counts as used until %s." % (
                    self.ledger, " ".join(str(error).split()),
                    policy.hhmm_utc(ledger.exhausted_until)))
        # A time ahead of now (a hand edit, a clock that went back) must
        # not freeze the budgets past the hour of spec 3.10.
        limit = wall + policy.CORRUPT_LEDGER_S
        if ledger.exhausted_until is not None \
                and ledger.exhausted_until > limit:
            ledger.exhausted_until = limit
        for booking in ledger.real + ledger.shadow:
            booking.wall = min(booking.wall, wall)
        return ledger, ""

    def save_ledger(self, ledger: policy.Ledger) -> None:
        """Write the ledger.

        raise (OSError): if it cannot be written, or if it holds a time
            that JSON cannot (NaN, an infinity): a failed write, which
            the callers handle as one.

        """
        try:
            text = ledger.to_json()
        except ValueError as error:
            raise OSError("the ledger holds a time that is not a number: "
                          "%s" % error) from error
        write_atomic(self.ledger, text)

    def save_status(self, status: Mapping) -> None:
        """Write the status file (raise OSError if it cannot be)."""
        write_atomic(self.status,
                     json.dumps(status, sort_keys=True, default=str))

    def append_journal(self, record: Mapping) -> None:
        """Append a line to the journal (raise OSError if it cannot)."""
        os.makedirs(self.evidence_dir, exist_ok=True)
        with open(self.journal, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(record, sort_keys=True, default=str) + "\n")

    def evidence_name(self, wall: float, program: str) -> str:
        """Return the name of an evidence file in evidence_dir."""
        return "%s-%s.json" % (
            time.strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ", time.gmtime(wall)),
            program.replace(" ", "_"))

    def write_evidence(self, name: str, evidence: Mapping) -> None:
        """Write an evidence file (raise OSError if it cannot be)."""
        write_atomic(os.path.join(self.evidence_dir, name),
                     json.dumps(evidence, indent=1, sort_keys=True,
                                default=str))


def log_record(level: str, text: str, wall: float) -> dict:
    """Return the keyword arguments of LogService.Log for an alert.

    LogService rebuilds a log record from them (logging.makeLogRecord),
    and the Overview's Logs table shows it as from "Watchdog,0".

    level: "WARNING" or "ERROR".
    text: the alert line.
    wall: when it happened (epoch seconds).

    return: the arguments.

    """
    return {"msg": text, "levelname": level, "levelno": LEVELS[level],
            "created": wall, "service_name": "Watchdog",
            "service_shard": 0, "operation": "", "exc_text": None}


class Alerter:
    """Write alerts to stdout and to LogService (3.9).

    stdout always (it reaches ./logs.sh). WARNING and ERROR lines also
    go to LogService, at the end of each cycle, best effort: on the
    first call that fails, the rest of that cycle's lines are left to
    stdout, so a stuck LogService costs one timeout per cycle.

    """

    def __init__(self, log_service: probes.RpcProbe | None,
                 out: TextIO | None = None):
        """Prepare the channels.

        log_service: an RpcProbe to LogService (one persistent
            connection of its own), or None.
        out: where to print (sys.stdout by default).

        """
        self._log_service = log_service
        self._out = out if out is not None else sys.stdout
        self._pending: list[dict] = []

    def emit(self, level: str, text: str, wall: float) -> None:
        """Print an alert, and queue it for LogService if WARNING+."""
        print("%s %s %s" % (utc(wall), level, text), file=self._out,
              flush=True)
        if self._log_service is not None \
                and LEVELS[level] >= LEVELS["WARNING"]:
            self._pending.append(log_record(level, text, wall))

    def resend(self, alerts: Iterable[policy.Decision], wall: float) -> None:
        """Queue the open alerts again, for a restarted LogService.

        A restarted LogService starts with an empty Overview table.

        alerts: the open alerts.
        wall: now (epoch seconds).

        """
        if self._log_service is None:
            return
        for alert in alerts:
            if LEVELS[alert.level] >= LEVELS["WARNING"]:
                self._pending.append(log_record(alert.level, alert.text,
                                                wall))

    async def flush(self) -> None:
        """Send the queued lines to LogService."""
        pending, self._pending = self._pending, []
        for record in pending:
            result = await self._log_service.call("Log", record)
            if not result.ok:
                break


def status_line(path: str, environ: Mapping[str, str], now: float) -> str:
    """Return the line of cmsWatchdog --status (3.10).

    path: the status file.
    environ: the environment (CMS_WATCHDOG comes from .env).
    now: the epoch time.

    return: the line.

    """
    if environ.get("CMS_WATCHDOG", "").strip().lower() == "off":
        return "watchdog: off (not configured)"
    try:
        with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            status = json.load(f)
        mode = status["mode"]
        since = max(0.0, now - float(status["time"]))
        open_alerts = len(status["open_alerts"])
    except FileNotFoundError:
        return "no status file"
    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as error:
        return "watchdog: the status file %s does not parse (%s)" % (
            path, " ".join(str(error).split()))
    line = "watchdog: %s, last cycle %d s ago, %d open alerts" % (
        mode, since, open_alerts)
    if status.get("supervisord") == "unreachable":
        line += ", supervisord unreachable"
    if since > 3 * policy.CYCLE_S:
        line += " (stale: is cmswatchdog running? See ./status.sh)"
    return line
```

- Modify: `setup.py` (`console_scripts`, after
  `"cmsTelegramBot=cmscontrib.TelegramBot:main",` at :155)
- Create: `cmstestsuite/unit_tests/watchdog/main_cycle_test.py`
  (asyncio group)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything of Tasks 8 to 16: `policy.decide`,
  `program_status`, `busy_sections`, the constants; `probes.http_probe`,
  `RpcProbe`, `ranking_target`, `db_gate`, `psql_environment`,
  `proc_facts`, `thread_cpu_s`; `control.SupervisorSource`,
  `ProcSource`, `ProcessSource`, `SignalActuator`, `DryRunActuator`,
  `Target`, `Outcome`; `Files`, `Alerter`, `status_line`, `utc`.
- Produces (in `cms/watchdog/main.py`):
  - `http_address(kind, shard) -> tuple[str, int] | None`,
    `rpc_address(kind, shard) -> tuple[str, int] | None` (from
    `cms.config`), and their type `AddressOf`;
  - `class Prober(gate_env, proc="/proc")` with `async http(address)`,
    `async rpc(address, method)`, `async ranking(target)`,
    `async gate()`, `proc_facts(pid)`, `thread_cpu_s(pid, tid)`,
    `close()`;
  - `restart_line(decision, program, outcome, evidence, shadow_count) ->
    tuple[str, str]` and `start_line(decision, outcome) -> tuple[str,
    str]` (level, line);
  - `class Watchdog(*, state, source: control.ProcessSource, actuator:
    control.DryRunActuator, dry_run: control.DryRunActuator, prober:
    Prober, files, alerter, rankings=(), rearm=..., status_history=None,
    http_address: AddressOf = http_address, rpc_address: AddressOf =
    rpc_address)` with
    `async cycle(mono, wall, lag_s, since_previous_s)`,
    `async run_forever(clock=time.monotonic, wall_clock=time.time,
    sleep=asyncio.sleep, cycles=None)`, `async settle()`;
  - `async serve(mode, restart_classes, process_source, files,
    status_history) -> int`;
  - `async take_snapshot(program, files, source: control.ProcessSource |
    None = None, prober: Prober | None = None, out: TextIO | None =
    None, rpc_address: AddressOf = rpc_address) -> int`; `program` is
    supervisord's program name, which the policy's alerts print after
    `cmsWatchdog --snapshot` (`cmscontestwebserver1`), or its label;
  - the evidence file (3.8): `time`, `mode`, `decision`,
    `observations`, `supervisor`, `health` (the last `health()` answer
    the watchdog received from the program, kept per program with its
    time) and `health_time`, `gate`, `snapshot`, `proc`;
  - `parse_classes(text: str) -> frozenset[str]` (raises `ValueError`);
  - `main(argv: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> int`, the
    `cmsWatchdog` console script;
  - the status file's keys: `time`, `time_utc`, `cycle`, `mode`,
    `restart_classes`, `cycle_duration_s`, `lag_s`, `starved`,
    `supervisord` (`"ok"` or `"unreachable"`), `gate` (`"green"` or
    `"red"`), `gate_reason`, `gate_detail`, `programs` (Task 11's
    `program_status`), `rankings`, `open_alerts`, `incidents`,
    `action_in_flight` (Task 21's harness reads them);
  - the journal's lines: a booking `{"time", "mode", "program",
    "label", "rule", "action", "would", "shadow", "pid", "evidence",
    "evidence_planned": true}` (for a start: `"programs"` instead of
    the program fields), then an outcome `{"time", "program" or
    "programs", "outcome", "detail", "new_pid", "killed", "usr1",
    "evidence_error"}`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** Create
  `cmstestsuite/unit_tests/watchdog/main_cycle_test.py`:

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for cmsWatchdog's cycle, actions, schedule and command line."""

import contextlib
import io
import json
import os
import shutil
import subprocess
import tempfile
import unittest
from dataclasses import replace
from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, patch

from cms.watchdog import main as watchdog
from cms.watchdog.control import Outcome, ProcessInfo, Target
from cms.watchdog.main import Alerter, Files, Watchdog, parse_classes, \
    restart_line, take_snapshot
from cms.watchdog.policy import ENFORCE, HOLDS, OBSERVE, RESTART, \
    Decision, PolicyState, ProgramFacts
from cms.watchdog.probes import GateResult, ProbeResult


WALL = 1_800_000_000.0
MONO = 10_000.0
ALL = ("web", "core", "infra", "workers")
KINDS = ("LogService", "EvaluationService", "Worker", "ContestWebServer")


def info(name: str, kind: str, shard: int, pid: int) -> ProcessInfo:
    return ProcessInfo(name, kind, shard, "RUNNING", pid, WALL - 86_400)


PROGRAMS = (info("cmslogservice", "LogService", 0, 101),
            info("cmsevaluationservice", "EvaluationService", 0, 104),
            info("cmsworker0", "Worker", 0, 105),
            info("cmscontestwebserver0", "ContestWebServer", 0, 108),
            info("cmscontestwebserver1", "ContestWebServer", 1, 109))


def http_address(kind: str, shard: int) -> tuple[str, int] | None:
    if kind != "ContestWebServer":
        return None
    return "127.0.0.1", 8000 + shard


def rpc_address(kind: str, shard: int) -> tuple[str, int]:
    return "127.0.0.1", 20_000 + 10 * KINDS.index(kind) + shard


CWS1 = {http_address("ContestWebServer", 1),
        rpc_address("ContestWebServer", 1)}
PASS = ProbeResult(True, 0.01)
FAIL = ProbeResult(False, None, "timed out")


class FakeSource:
    can_act = True

    def __init__(self):
        self.infos = list(PROGRAMS)
        self.down = False

    async def programs(self) -> list[ProcessInfo]:
        if self.down:
            raise TimeoutError("timed out")
        return list(self.infos)

    def set(self, name: str, **changes) -> None:
        self.infos = [replace(item, **changes) if item.name == name
                      else item for item in self.infos]


class FakeProber:
    """Every probe passes, unless its address is in failing."""

    def __init__(self):
        self.failing: set = set()
        self.gate_result = GateResult(True)
        self.calls: list = []

    async def http(self, address):
        return FAIL if address in self.failing else PASS

    async def rpc(self, address, method):
        self.calls.append((address, method))
        if address in self.failing:
            return FAIL
        if method == "workers_status":
            return ProbeResult(True, 0.01, data={"0": {
                "connected": True, "disabled_by_timeout": False,
                "disabled_at": None}})
        return ProbeResult(True, 0.01, data={"uptime_s": 9_999.0,
                                             "busy": []})

    async def ranking(self, target):
        return PASS

    async def gate(self):
        return self.gate_result

    def proc_facts(self, pid):
        return {"state": "S", "wchan": "do_epoll_wait",
                "threads": {pid: "S"}, "cpu_s": 1.0}

    def thread_cpu_s(self, pid, tid):
        return 1.0

    def close(self):
        pass


class FakeLog:
    def __init__(self):
        self.calls: list = []

    async def call(self, method, data=None):
        self.calls.append((method, data))
        return PASS


class FakeActuator:
    """Books, writes the evidence, and returns its outcome."""

    def __init__(self, outcome: Outcome):
        self.outcome = outcome
        self.calls: list = []

    async def restart(self, target, book, write_evidence):
        book()
        write_evidence()
        self.calls.append(target)
        return self.outcome

    async def start(self, names, book):
        book()
        self.calls.append(tuple(names))
        return Outcome("started")


class Rig:
    """A Watchdog over fakes, in temporary directories."""

    def __init__(self, test: unittest.TestCase, mode: str = ENFORCE,
                 restart: tuple = ALL):
        root = tempfile.mkdtemp()
        test.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, root)
        self.files = Files(os.path.join(root, "run"),
                           os.path.join(root, "log"))
        self.out = io.StringIO()
        self.log = FakeLog()
        self.source = FakeSource()
        self.prober = FakeProber()
        self.actuator = FakeActuator(Outcome("restarted", new_pid=999))
        self.dry_run = FakeActuator(Outcome(
            "would", usr1="no USR1: the process does not handle it"))
        self.rearms: list = []
        self.history = os.path.join(root, "history.jsonl")
        self.watchdog = Watchdog(
            state=PolicyState(mode=mode, restart_classes=frozenset(restart)),
            source=self.source, actuator=self.actuator,
            dry_run=self.dry_run, prober=self.prober, files=self.files,
            alerter=Alerter(self.log, self.out),
            rearm=lambda: self.rearms.append(True),
            status_history=self.history, http_address=http_address,
            rpc_address=rpc_address)
        self.mono, self.wall, self.count = MONO, WALL, 0

    async def cycle(self, lag_s: float = 0.0) -> None:
        if self.count:
            self.mono += 15.0
            self.wall += 15.0
        await self.watchdog.cycle(self.mono, self.wall, lag_s,
                                  15.0 if self.count else None)
        self.count += 1
        await self.watchdog.settle()

    async def trip_cws1(self) -> None:
        """Run 9 passing cycles, then 5 in which CWS 1 fails."""
        for _ in range(9):
            await self.cycle()
        self.prober.failing = set(CWS1)
        for _ in range(5):
            await self.cycle()

    def lines(self) -> list[str]:
        return self.out.getvalue().splitlines()

    def status(self) -> dict:
        with open(self.files.status) as f:
            return json.load(f)

    def journal(self) -> list[dict]:
        with open(self.files.journal) as f:
            return [json.loads(line) for line in f]


class TestCycle(unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase):

    async def test_one_full_cycle(self):
        rig = Rig(self)
        await rig.cycle()
        status = rig.status()
        self.assertEqual(
            (status["mode"], status["supervisord"], status["gate"],
             status["cycle"]), ("enforce", "ok", "green", 0))
        self.assertEqual([entry["name"] for entry in status["programs"]],
                         [item.name for item in PROGRAMS])
        self.assertTrue(os.path.exists(rig.files.ledger))
        self.assertEqual(len(rig.rearms), 1)
        with open(rig.history) as f:
            self.assertEqual(len(f.readlines()), 1)
        self.assertIn((rpc_address("EvaluationService", 0),
                       "workers_status"), rig.prober.calls)
        self.assertEqual(rig.lines(), [])

    async def test_supervisord_unreachable(self):
        rig = Rig(self)
        await rig.cycle()
        rig.source.down = True
        await rig.cycle()
        status = rig.status()
        self.assertEqual(status["supervisord"], "unreachable")
        self.assertEqual(len(status["programs"]), len(PROGRAMS))

    async def test_the_rate_limit_and_the_resend_to_a_new_logservice(self):
        rig = Rig(self)
        rig.prober.gate_result = GateResult(False, "refused",
                                            "Connection refused")
        await rig.cycle()
        await rig.cycle()
        red = [line for line in rig.lines() if "gate is red" in line]
        self.assertEqual(len(red), 1)
        self.assertEqual(len(rig.log.calls), 1)
        rig.source.set("cmslogservice", pid=201)
        await rig.cycle()
        self.assertEqual(
            len([line for line in rig.lines() if "gate is red" in line]), 1)
        messages = [data["msg"] for _, data in rig.log.calls]
        self.assertEqual(len(messages), 2)
        self.assertEqual(messages[0], messages[1])

    async def test_the_self_guard_is_rearmed_every_cycle(self):
        rig = Rig(self)
        for _ in range(3):
            await rig.cycle()
        self.assertEqual(len(rig.rearms), 3)

    async def test_a_restart(self):
        rig = Rig(self)
        await rig.trip_cws1()
        self.assertEqual(rig.actuator.calls, [
            Target("cmscontestwebserver1", "ContestWebServer", 109)])
        with open(rig.files.ledger) as f:
            self.assertEqual([b["program"] for b in json.load(f)["real"]],
                             ["cmscontestwebserver1"])
        booking, outcome = rig.journal()
        self.assertEqual((booking["action"], booking["evidence_planned"]),
                         ("restart", True))
        self.assertEqual(outcome["outcome"], "restarted")
        name = os.path.basename(booking["evidence"])
        with open(os.path.join(rig.files.evidence_dir, name)) as f:
            evidence = json.load(f)
        self.assertEqual(evidence["decision"]["rule"], "R1")
        self.assertEqual(evidence["decision"]["holds_checked"], list(HOLDS))
        self.assertEqual(len(evidence["observations"]), 6)
        self.assertEqual(evidence["supervisor"]["pid"], 109)
        # Its probes failed in the trip's cycles: the evidence keeps the
        # last health() answer received, from the 9th cycle.
        self.assertEqual(evidence["health"],
                         {"uptime_s": 9_999.0, "busy": []})
        self.assertEqual(evidence["health_time"],
                         watchdog.utc(WALL + 8 * 15))
        self.assertEqual(evidence["proc"]["wchan"], "do_epoll_wait")
        self.assertIn(
            "WARNING Watchdog: restarted cmsContestWebServer 1 (pid "
            "109→999) after 5 of its last 6 favicon probes failed (R1); "
            "DB OK; evidence log/watchdog/%s; stacks in ./logs.sh." % name,
            rig.lines()[-1])

    async def test_a_would_restart_in_observe_mode(self):
        rig = Rig(self, mode=OBSERVE)
        await rig.trip_cws1()
        self.assertEqual(rig.actuator.calls, [])
        self.assertEqual(len(rig.dry_run.calls), 1)
        line = rig.lines()[-1]
        self.assertIn("Watchdog: WOULD restart cmsContestWebServer 1 (pid "
                      "109) after 5 of its last 6 favicon probes failed "
                      "(R1); DB OK; evidence log/watchdog/", line)
        self.assertTrue(line.endswith(
            "no USR1: the process does not handle it; observe mode: "
            "nothing was restarted."))

    async def test_a_would_restart_of_a_class_not_enabled(self):
        rig = Rig(self, restart=("core", "infra"))
        await rig.trip_cws1()
        self.assertIn("class web is not in CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART (H9), so "
                      "nothing was done; shadow count 1 of 2 this hour.",
                      rig.lines()[-1])

    async def test_an_unwritable_status_file_alerts_and_the_cycle_goes_on(
            self):
        rig = Rig(self)
        # A directory where the file goes: the rename fails, even as root.
        os.makedirs(rig.files.status)
        await rig.cycle()
        self.assertTrue(any("status file" in line
                            and "could not be written" in line
                            for line in rig.lines()))
        self.assertEqual(len(rig.rearms), 1)
        self.assertTrue(os.path.exists(rig.files.ledger))

    async def test_the_evidence_directory_is_created(self):
        rig = Rig(self, mode=OBSERVE)
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(rig.files.evidence_dir))
        await rig.trip_cws1()
        self.assertEqual(len([name for name in
                              os.listdir(rig.files.evidence_dir)
                              if name.endswith(".json")]), 1)

    async def test_the_schedule_measures_lag_and_pauses(self):
        rig = Rig(self)
        seen: list = []

        async def cycle(mono, wall, lag_s, since_previous_s):
            seen.append((lag_s, since_previous_s))

        now = [1000.0]
        sleeps: list = []

        async def sleep(delay):
            sleeps.append(delay)
            # The second sleep lasts 45 s more: a pause.
            now[0] += delay + (45.0 if len(sleeps) == 2 else 0.0)

        rig.watchdog.cycle = cycle
        await rig.watchdog.run_forever(clock=lambda: now[0],
                                       wall_clock=lambda: WALL + now[0],
                                       sleep=sleep, cycles=4)
        self.assertEqual(seen, [(0.0, None), (0.0, 15.0), (45.0, 60.0),
                                (0.0, 15.0)])


class TestSnapshot(unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase):

    async def asyncSetUp(self):
        root = tempfile.mkdtemp()
        self.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, root)
        self.files = Files(os.path.join(root, "run"),
                           os.path.join(root, "log"))
        self.source = FakeSource()

    async def snapshot(self, program: str) -> tuple[int, str]:
        out = io.StringIO()
        code = await take_snapshot(program, self.files, source=self.source,
                                   prober=FakeProber(), out=out,
                                   rpc_address=rpc_address)
        return code, out.getvalue()

    async def test_snapshot_of_an_unknown_program_exits_1(self):
        code, text = await self.snapshot("cmsworker9")
        self.assertEqual(code, 1)
        self.assertIn("no RUNNING CMS program named 'cmsworker9'", text)
        self.source.set("cmscontestwebserver1", state="STOPPED", pid=None)
        code, text = await self.snapshot("cmscontestwebserver1")
        self.assertEqual(code, 1)
        self.assertIn("it is STOPPED", text)
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(self.files.evidence_dir))

    async def test_a_snapshot_of_a_running_program(self):
        sleeper = subprocess.Popen(["sleep", "30"])
        self.addCleanup(sleeper.wait)
        self.addCleanup(sleeper.kill)
        self.source.set("cmscontestwebserver0", pid=sleeper.pid)
        # The supervisord name, which the alerts print after
        # "cmsWatchdog --snapshot", and the label.
        for program in ("cmscontestwebserver0", "cmsContestWebServer 0"):
            with self.subTest(program=program):
                code, text = await self.snapshot(program)
                self.assertEqual(code, 0, text)
                [name] = [name for name in os.listdir(
                    self.files.evidence_dir) if name in text]
                self.assertIn("no USR1: the process does not handle it",
                              text)
                with open(os.path.join(self.files.evidence_dir,
                                       name)) as f:
                    self.assertEqual(json.load(f)["supervisor"]["pid"],
                                     sleeper.pid)
        self.assertIsNone(sleeper.poll())
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(self.files.journal))


class TestCommandLine(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        root = tempfile.mkdtemp()
        self.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, root)
        patcher = patch.object(watchdog, "config")
        config = patcher.start()
        self.addCleanup(patcher.stop)
        config.global_.run_dir = os.path.join(root, "run")
        config.global_.log_dir = os.path.join(root, "log")

    def run_main(self, *argv: str) -> tuple[int, str]:
        out = io.StringIO()
        with contextlib.redirect_stdout(out):
            code = watchdog.main(list(argv))
        return code, out.getvalue()

    def test_status(self):
        with patch.dict(os.environ, {"CMS_WATCHDOG": "observe"}):
            self.assertEqual(self.run_main("--status"),
                             (0, "no status file\n"))
        with patch.dict(os.environ, {"CMS_WATCHDOG": "off"}):
            self.assertEqual(self.run_main("--status"),
                             (0, "watchdog: off (not configured)\n"))

    def test_the_proc_source_forces_observe_mode(self):
        with patch.object(watchdog, "serve",
                          new=AsyncMock(return_value=0)) as serve, \
                contextlib.redirect_stderr(io.StringIO()):
            code, _ = self.run_main("--process-source", "proc", "--mode",
                                    "enforce")
        self.assertEqual(code, 0)
        self.assertEqual(serve.await_args.args[:3],
                         (OBSERVE, frozenset({"web", "core", "infra"}),
                          "proc"))

    def test_restart_classes(self):
        self.assertEqual(parse_classes("Web, core,,web"),
                         frozenset({"web", "core"}))
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            parse_classes("web,nope")
        with self.assertRaises(SystemExit), \
                contextlib.redirect_stderr(io.StringIO()):
            self.run_main("--restart", "nope")


class TestLines(unittest.TestCase):

    PROGRAM = ProgramFacts("cmscontestwebserver1", "ContestWebServer", 1,
                           "RUNNING", 109, WALL)
    DECISION = Decision(RESTART, text="5 of its last 6 favicon probes "
                        "failed", program="cmscontestwebserver1", rule="R1")

    def test_restart_lines(self):
        cases = (
            (Outcome("restarted", new_pid=999, killed=True), "WARNING",
             "Watchdog: restarted cmsContestWebServer 1 (pid 109→999, KILL "
             "after 10 s) after 5 of its last 6 favicon probes failed "
             "(R1); DB OK; evidence log/watchdog/e.json; stacks in "
             "./logs.sh."),
            (Outcome("unconfirmed", "no new pid was RUNNING within 30 s",
                     killed=True), "ERROR",
             "Watchdog: sent TERM and KILL to cmsContestWebServer 1 (pid "
             "109) after 5 of its last 6 favicon probes failed (R1), but no "
             "new pid was RUNNING within 30 s. Check ./status.sh: a program "
             "left FATAL is started again only after a database outage."),
            (Outcome("unbooked", "its ledger could not be written: disk "
                     "full"), "ERROR",
             "Watchdog: did not restart cmsContestWebServer 1: its ledger "
             "could not be written: disk full, and an unbooked restart "
             "would bypass the budget. Check the free space of the run "
             "directory."),
            (Outcome("refused", "it is no longer RUNNING as pid 109"),
             "WARNING",
             "Watchdog: did not restart cmsContestWebServer 1: it is no "
             "longer RUNNING as pid 109; nothing was signalled."),
        )
        for outcome, level, line in cases:
            with self.subTest(result=outcome.result):
                self.assertEqual(
                    restart_line(self.DECISION, self.PROGRAM, outcome,
                                 "e.json", 0), (level, line))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```
