```python


def http_address(kind: str, shard: int) -> tuple[str, int] | None:
    """Return where a CWS or AWS serves HTTP (3.2), or None.

    kind, shard: the program.

    return: (host, port); a wildcard address is probed on loopback.

    """
    if kind == "ContestWebServer":
        ports = config.contest_web_server.listen_port
        addresses = config.contest_web_server.listen_address
        if shard >= len(ports):
            return None
        address = addresses[shard] if shard < len(addresses) else ""
        return probes.probe_host(address), ports[shard]
    if kind == "AdminWebServer":
        return (probes.probe_host(config.admin_web_server.listen_address),
                config.admin_web_server.listen_port)
    return None


def rpc_address(kind: str, shard: int) -> tuple[str, int] | None:
    """Return a service's RPC address from cms.toml (3.2), or None."""
    address = config.services.get(ServiceCoord(kind, shard))
    if address is None:
        return None
    return probes.probe_host(address.ip), address.port


# Where a program listens: http_address or rpc_address (tests pass
# fakes).
AddressOf = Callable[[str, int], tuple[str, int] | None]


class Prober:
    """The probes of one watchdog, with their persistent connections."""

    def __init__(self, gate_env: Mapping[str, str], proc: str = "/proc"):
        """Prepare the probes.

        gate_env: the environment of the gate's psql.
        proc: where /proc is.

        """
        self._gate_env = dict(gate_env)
        self._proc = proc
        self._rpc: dict[tuple[str, int], probes.RpcProbe] = {}

    async def http(self, address: tuple[str, int]) -> probes.ProbeResult:
        """Probe a web server's favicon (R1)."""
        return await probes.http_probe(address[0], address[1],
                                       "/static/favicon.ico",
                                       policy.HTTP_TIMEOUT_S)

    async def rpc(self, address: tuple[str, int],
                  method: str) -> probes.ProbeResult:
        """Call method on the service's persistent connection."""
        probe = self._rpc.get(address)
        if probe is None:
            probe = self._rpc[address] = probes.RpcProbe(
                address[0], address[1], policy.RPC_TIMEOUT_S)
        return await probe.call(method)

    async def ranking(self,
                      target: probes.RankingTarget) -> probes.ProbeResult:
        """Probe a ranking: any status line passes, no credentials."""
        return await probes.http_probe(target.host, target.port, "/",
                                       policy.RANKING_TIMEOUT_S,
                                       any_status=True, tls=target.tls)

    async def gate(self) -> probes.GateResult:
        """Run the database gate."""
        return await probes.db_gate(self._gate_env, policy.GATE_KILL_S)

    def proc_facts(self, pid: int) -> dict | None:
        return probes.proc_facts(pid, self._proc)

    def thread_cpu_s(self, pid: int, tid: int) -> float | None:
        return probes.thread_cpu_s(pid, tid, self._proc)

    def close(self) -> None:
        """Close every persistent connection."""
        for probe in self._rpc.values():
            probe.close()


async def _gather(jobs: Mapping[str, Awaitable]) -> dict:
    """Await the jobs concurrently; return their results by key."""
    results = await asyncio.gather(*jobs.values())
    return dict(zip(jobs, results))


def _probe(result: probes.ProbeResult) -> policy.Probe:
    return policy.Probe(result.ok, result.latency_s, result.error)


def _gate(result: probes.GateResult) -> policy.Gate:
    return policy.Gate(result.green, result.reason, result.detail,
                       tuple(policy.Backend(**row)
                             for row in result.backends))


def restart_line(decision: policy.Decision, program: policy.ProgramFacts,
                 outcome: control.Outcome, evidence: str,
                 shadow_count: int) -> tuple[str, str]:
    """Return the level and the line that report a restart (3.9).

    decision: the restart decision.
    program: the program, as the cycle saw it.
    outcome: what the actuator did.
    evidence: the evidence file's name.
    shadow_count: the program's restarts in the shadow ledger (H9).

    return: (level, line).

    """
    where = "evidence log/watchdog/%s" % evidence
    what = "%s (%s)" % (decision.text, decision.rule)
    if outcome.result == "restarted":
        return "WARNING", (
            "Watchdog: restarted %s (pid %d→%d%s) after %s; DB OK; %s; "
            "stacks in ./logs.sh." % (
                program.label, program.pid, outcome.new_pid,
                ", KILL after %d s" % policy.TERM_WAIT_S
                if outcome.killed else "", what, where))
    if outcome.result == "would":
        if decision.shadow:
            tail = ("class %s is not in CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART (H9), so "
                    "nothing was done; shadow count %d of %d this hour."
                    % (program.klass, shadow_count, policy.PROGRAM_BUDGET))
        else:
            tail = "observe mode: nothing was restarted."
        return "WARNING", (
            "Watchdog: WOULD restart %s (pid %d) after %s; DB OK; %s; %s; "
            "%s" % (program.label, program.pid, what, where, outcome.usr1,
                    tail))
    if outcome.result == "unconfirmed":
        return "ERROR", (
            "Watchdog: sent TERM%s to %s (pid %d) after %s, but %s. Check "
            "./status.sh: a program left FATAL is started again only "
            "after a database outage." % (
                " and KILL" if outcome.killed else "", program.label,
                program.pid, what, outcome.detail))
    if outcome.result == "unbooked":
        return "ERROR", (
            "Watchdog: did not restart %s: %s, and an unbooked restart "
            "would bypass the budget. Check the free space of the run "
            "directory." % (program.label, outcome.detail))
    return "WARNING", ("Watchdog: did not restart %s: %s; nothing was "
                       "signalled." % (program.label, outcome.detail))


def start_line(decision: policy.Decision,
               outcome: control.Outcome) -> tuple[str, str]:
    """Return the level and the line that report an R5 batch."""
    names = ", ".join(decision.programs)
    if outcome.result == "started":
        return "WARNING", ("Watchdog: started %s, left FATAL by a database "
                           "outage (R5). Check ./status.sh." % names)
    if outcome.result == "would":
        tail = ("their class is not in CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART (H9), so "
                "nothing was done." if decision.shadow
                else "observe mode: nothing was started.")
        return "WARNING", ("Watchdog: WOULD start %s, left FATAL by a "
                           "database outage (R5); %s" % (names, tail))
    if outcome.result == "unbooked":
        return "ERROR", "Watchdog: did not start %s: %s." % (
            names, outcome.detail)
    return "ERROR", ("Watchdog: could not start all of %s: %s. Read "
                     "./logs.sh, then start them with supervisorctl."
                     % (names, outcome.detail))


class Watchdog:
    """One cmsWatchdog: its cycle, its actions and its files (3.3)."""

    def __init__(self, *, state: policy.PolicyState,
                 source: control.ProcessSource,
                 actuator: control.DryRunActuator,
                 dry_run: control.DryRunActuator, prober: Prober,
                 files: Files, alerter: Alerter,
                 rankings: Sequence[probes.RankingTarget] = (),
                 rearm: Callable[[], None] = lambda: None,
                 status_history: str | None = None,
                 http_address: AddressOf = http_address,
                 rpc_address: AddressOf = rpc_address):
        """Wire the watchdog.

        state: the policy state (with the ledger read at startup).
        source: the process source.
        actuator: the actuator of real actions (a SignalActuator, or a
            DryRunActuator when the source cannot act).
        dry_run: the actuator of would-actions.
        prober: a Prober (or a fake with the same methods).
        files: where the state goes.
        alerter: where the alerts go.
        rankings: the rankings to probe.
        rearm: re-arms the self-guard; called at the end of each cycle.
        status_history: a file to append each cycle's status to, or
            None.
        http_address, rpc_address: where each program listens.

        """
        self.state = state
        self.source = source
        self.actuator = actuator
        self.dry_run = dry_run
        self.prober = prober
        self.files = files
        self.alerter = alerter
        self.rankings = list(rankings)
        self.rearm = rearm
        self.status_history = status_history
        self._http_address = http_address
        self._rpc_address = rpc_address
        self.cycles = 0
        self._programs: tuple[policy.ProgramFacts, ...] = ()
        self._action: asyncio.Task | None = None
        self._log_service_pid: int | None = None
        self._cpu: dict[int, float] = {}
        self._proc: dict[str, dict | None] = {}
        # By program name: the last health() answer received and its
        # time (epoch seconds), for the evidence (3.8). A trip of R1 or
        # R2 has no answer in its own cycle.
        self._last_health: dict[str, tuple[float, Mapping]] = {}
        self._file_alerts: dict[str, float] = {}

    async def run_forever(self, clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,
                          wall_clock: Callable[[], float] = time.time,
                          sleep: Callable[[float], Awaitable[None]] =
                          asyncio.sleep,
                          cycles: int | None = None) -> None:
        """Run a cycle every CYCLE_S seconds on a monotonic schedule.

        Each cycle gets its lag (how late it started) and the time since
        the previous one: a starved or paused watchdog tells the policy.
        A schedule left behind starts again from now.

        clock, wall_clock, sleep: time.monotonic, time.time and
            asyncio.sleep (tests pass fakes).
        cycles: stop after that many cycles (tests); None runs forever.

        """
        scheduled = clock()
        previous = None
        count = 0
        while cycles is None or count < cycles:
            start = clock()
            await self.cycle(start, wall_clock(), max(0.0, start - scheduled),
                             None if previous is None else start - previous)
            count += 1
            previous = start
            scheduled += policy.CYCLE_S
            if scheduled < clock():
                scheduled = clock() + policy.CYCLE_S
            await sleep(max(0.0, scheduled - clock()))

    async def settle(self) -> None:
        """Wait for the action in flight, if there is one."""
        if self._action is not None:
            await self._action

    async def cycle(self, mono: float, wall: float, lag_s: float,
                    since_previous_s: float | None) -> None:
        """Run one cycle (3.3): probe, decide, act, record.

        mono, wall: the monotonic and the epoch time of its start.
        lag_s: how late it started.
        since_previous_s: since the previous cycle, None for the first.

        """
        started = time.monotonic()
        programs, supervisor_ok = await self._read_programs()
        running = [program for program in programs
                   if program.managed and program.state == "RUNNING"]
        http_jobs, rpc_jobs = {}, {}
        for program in running:
            address = self._http_address(program.kind, program.shard)
            if program.kind in policy.HTTP_KINDS and address is not None:
                http_jobs[program.name] = self.prober.http(address)
            address = self._rpc_address(program.kind, program.shard)
            if program.kind in policy.HEALTH_KINDS and address is not None:
                rpc_jobs[program.name] = self.prober.rpc(address, "health")
        ranking_jobs = {target.visible: self.prober.ranking(target)
                        for target in self.rankings}
        gate, http, rpc, rankings = await asyncio.gather(
            self.prober.gate(), _gather(http_jobs), _gather(rpc_jobs),
            _gather(ranking_jobs))
        workers, holder_cpu = await self._es_extras(running, rpc)
        observation = policy.Observation(
            lag_s=lag_s, since_previous_s=since_previous_s,
            supervisor_ok=supervisor_ok, programs=programs,
            gate=_gate(gate),
            http={name: _probe(result) for name, result in http.items()},
            rpc={name: _probe(result) for name, result in rpc.items()},
            health={name: result.data for name, result in rpc.items()
                    if result.ok and isinstance(result.data, Mapping)},
            workers=workers,
            rankings={address: _probe(result)
                      for address, result in rankings.items()},
            holder_cpu=holder_cpu,
            in_flight=self._action is not None and not self._action.done())
        self.state, decisions = policy.decide(self.state, observation, mono,
                                              wall)
        names = {program.name for program in running}
        self._last_health = {name: entry for name, entry
                             in self._last_health.items() if name in names}
        for name, payload in observation.health.items():
            self._last_health[name] = (wall, payload)
        self._proc = {program.name: self._proc_facts(program)
                      for program in running}
        live = {program.pid for program in running}
        self._cpu = {pid: cpu for pid, cpu in self._cpu.items()
                     if pid in live}
        actions = []
        for decision in decisions:
            if decision.action == policy.ALERT:
                self.alerter.emit(decision.level, decision.text, wall)
            else:
                actions.append(decision)
        if actions:
            self._action = asyncio.create_task(
                self._act(actions, observation, wall))
        self._check_log_service(programs, wall)
        self._record(wall, lag_s, observation, started)
        await self.alerter.flush()
        self.cycles += 1
        self.rearm()

    async def _read_programs(
            self) -> tuple[tuple[policy.ProgramFacts, ...], bool]:
        """Return the programs, and whether the source answered (H1).

        When supervisord does not answer, the last programs it gave are
        probed and judged, and H1 holds every trip.

        """
        try:
            infos = await self.source.programs()
        except Exception:
            return self._programs, False
        self._programs = tuple(policy.ProgramFacts(**asdict(info))
                               for info in infos)
        return self._programs, True

    async def _es_extras(
            self, running: list[policy.ProgramFacts],
            rpc: Mapping[str, probes.ProbeResult],
    ) -> tuple[Mapping | None, tuple[int, float] | None]:
        """Ask ES for its Workers and read its holder's CPU (R3, R4).

        return: (workers_status() or None, (tid, cpu_s) or None).

        """
        es = next((program for program in running
                   if program.kind == "EvaluationService"), None)
        if es is None or es.name not in rpc or not rpc[es.name].ok:
            return None, None
        answer = await self.prober.rpc(
            self._rpc_address(es.kind, es.shard), "workers_status")
        workers = (answer.data if answer.ok
                   and isinstance(answer.data, Mapping) else None)
        sections = policy.busy_sections(rpc[es.name].data)
        if not sections or sections[0]["tid"] is None:
            return workers, None
        tid = sections[0]["tid"]
        cpu_s = self.prober.thread_cpu_s(es.pid, tid)
        return workers, None if cpu_s is None else (tid, cpu_s)

    def _proc_facts(self, program: policy.ProgramFacts) -> dict | None:
        """Return the /proc facts of program, with its CPU delta.

        cpu_delta_s is its CPU time since the previous cycle, None on
        its first.

        """
        facts = self.prober.proc_facts(program.pid)
        if facts is None:
            return None
        previous = self._cpu.get(program.pid)
        facts = dict(facts, cpu_delta_s=None if previous is None
                     else facts["cpu_s"] - previous)
        self._cpu[program.pid] = facts["cpu_s"]
        return facts

    async def _act(self, decisions: list[policy.Decision],
                   observation: policy.Observation, wall: float) -> None:
        """Carry out the actions of one cycle, in order (3.7)."""
        for decision in decisions:
            try:
                if decision.action == policy.RESTART:
                    await self._restart(decision, observation, wall)
                else:
                    await self._start(decision, wall)
            except Exception as error:
                self.alerter.emit(
                    "ERROR", "Watchdog: the %s of %s failed unexpectedly: "
                    "%s" % (decision.action,
                            decision.program or ", ".join(decision.programs),
                            probes.describe(error)), time.time())

    async def _restart(self, decision: policy.Decision,
                       observation: policy.Observation, wall: float) -> None:
        """Restart, or would-restart, one program (3.7)."""
        program = next(item for item in observation.programs
                       if item.name == decision.program)
        name = self.files.evidence_name(wall, program.name)
        actuator = self.dry_run if decision.would else self.actuator

        def book() -> None:
            # An OSError here stops the action: an unbooked restart
            # would bypass the budget.
            self.files.save_ledger(self.state.ledger)
            self._journal({
                "time": utc(wall), "mode": self.state.mode,
                "program": program.name, "label": program.label,
                "rule": decision.rule, "action": decision.action,
                "would": decision.would, "shadow": decision.shadow,
                "pid": program.pid, "evidence": "log/watchdog/" + name,
                "evidence_planned": True}, wall)

        def write_evidence() -> None:
            self.files.write_evidence(
                name, self._evidence(decision, program, observation, wall))

        outcome = await actuator.restart(
            control.Target(program.name, program.kind, program.pid), book,
            write_evidence)
        now = time.time()
        self._journal({
            "time": utc(now), "program": program.name,
            "outcome": outcome.result, "detail": outcome.detail,
            "new_pid": outcome.new_pid, "killed": outcome.killed,
            "usr1": outcome.usr1, "evidence_error": outcome.evidence_error},
            now)
        level, text = restart_line(
            decision, program, outcome, name, self.state.ledger.count(
                program.name, policy.RESTART, True, wall))
        self.alerter.emit(level, text, now)
        if outcome.evidence_error:
            self.alerter.emit(
                "ERROR", "Watchdog: the evidence of %s could not be "
                "written (%s); the action went on. Check the free space of "
                "the log volume." % (program.label, outcome.evidence_error),
                now)

    async def _start(self, decision: policy.Decision, wall: float) -> None:
        """Start, or would-start, an R5 batch (3.7)."""
        actuator = self.dry_run if decision.would else self.actuator

        def book() -> None:
            self.files.save_ledger(self.state.ledger)
            self._journal({
                "time": utc(wall), "mode": self.state.mode,
                "programs": list(decision.programs), "rule": decision.rule,
                "action": decision.action, "would": decision.would,
                "shadow": decision.shadow}, wall)

        outcome = await actuator.start(decision.programs, book)
        now = time.time()
        self._journal({"time": utc(now), "programs": list(decision.programs),
                       "outcome": outcome.result, "detail": outcome.detail},
                      now)
        level, text = start_line(decision, outcome)
        self.alerter.emit(level, text, now)

    def _evidence(self, decision: policy.Decision,
                  program: policy.ProgramFacts,
                  observation: policy.Observation, wall: float) -> dict:
        """Return the evidence of an action (3.8).

        Its "health" is the last health() answer received from the
        program, at "health_time": an R1 or R2 trip has none in its own
        cycle.

        """
        program_state = self.state.programs.get(program.name)
        last_health = self._last_health.get(program.name)
        return {
            "time": utc(wall),
            "mode": self.state.mode,
            "decision": {
                "action": decision.action, "rule": decision.rule,
                "detail": decision.text, "would": decision.would,
                "shadow": decision.shadow, "holds_checked": list(policy.HOLDS),
                "holds_applied": ["H9"] if decision.shadow else []},
            "observations": list(program_state.history)
            if program_state is not None else [],
            "supervisor": asdict(program),
            "health": None if last_health is None else last_health[1],
            "health_time": None if last_health is None
            else utc(last_health[0]),
            "gate": {"green": observation.gate.green,
                     "reason": observation.gate.reason,
                     "detail": observation.gate.detail},
            "snapshot": [asdict(backend)
                         for backend in observation.gate.backends],
            "proc": self._proc.get(program.name),
        }

    def _journal(self, record: Mapping, wall: float) -> None:
        """Append to the journal; a failure alerts and goes on."""
        try:
            self.files.append_journal(record)
        except OSError as error:
            self._file_alert(
                "journal", "Watchdog: its journal %s could not be written "
                "(%s); actions go on. Check the free space of the log "
                "volume." % (self.files.journal, error), wall)

    def _file_alert(self, kind: str, text: str, wall: float) -> None:
        """Alert about a file, at most once per ALERT_REPEAT_S."""
        now = time.monotonic()
        last = self._file_alerts.get(kind)
        if last is None or now - last >= policy.ALERT_REPEAT_S:
            self._file_alerts[kind] = now
            self.alerter.emit("ERROR", text, wall)

    def _check_log_service(self, programs: Iterable[policy.ProgramFacts],
                           wall: float) -> None:
        """Send the open alerts again when LogService gets a new pid."""
        pid = next((program.pid for program in programs
                    if program.kind == "LogService"
                    and program.state == "RUNNING"), None)
        if pid is not None and self._log_service_pid is not None \
                and pid != self._log_service_pid:
            self.alerter.resend(self.state.open_alerts.values(), wall)
        if pid is not None:
            self._log_service_pid = pid

    def _record(self, wall: float, lag_s: float,
                observation: policy.Observation, started: float) -> None:
        """Write the status (and its history) and the ledger (3.10)."""
        status = {
            "time": wall, "time_utc": utc(wall), "cycle": self.cycles,
            "mode": self.state.mode,
            "restart_classes": sorted(self.state.restart_classes),
            "cycle_duration_s": time.monotonic() - started,
            "lag_s": lag_s, "starved": lag_s > policy.STARVED_LAG_S,
            "supervisord": "ok" if observation.supervisor_ok
            else "unreachable",
            "gate": "green" if observation.gate.green else "red",
            "gate_reason": observation.gate.reason,
            "gate_detail": observation.gate.detail,
            "programs": policy.program_status(self.state, observation,
                                              wall),
            "rankings": {address: asdict(probe) for address, probe
                         in observation.rankings.items()},
            "open_alerts": [alert.text for alert
                            in self.state.open_alerts.values()],
            "incidents": self.state.ledger.incidents,
            "action_in_flight": self._action is not None
            and not self._action.done(),
        }
        try:
            self.files.save_status(status)
        except OSError as error:
            self._file_alert(
                "status", "Watchdog: its status file %s could not be "
                "written (%s); it goes on, and ./status.sh shows a stale "
                "heartbeat meanwhile." % (self.files.status, error), wall)
        if self.status_history is not None:
            try:
                with open(self.status_history, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                    f.write(json.dumps(status, sort_keys=True, default=str)
                            + "\n")
            except OSError as error:
                self._file_alert(
                    "history", "Watchdog: its status history %s could not "
                    "be written (%s)." % (self.status_history, error), wall)
        try:
            self.files.save_ledger(self.state.ledger)
        except OSError as error:
            self._file_alert(
                "ledger", "Watchdog: its ledger %s could not be written "
                "(%s); a restart stops at its booking until it can be."
                % (self.files.ledger, error), wall)


def _gate_environment() -> dict[str, str]:
    """Return the environment of the gate's psql, from cms.toml."""
    return probes.psql_environment(
        config.database.url, os.environ.get("PATH", os.defpath),
        policy.GATE_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_S, policy.GATE_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS)


async def serve(mode: str, restart_classes: frozenset[str],
                process_source: str, files: Files,
                status_history: str | None) -> int:
    """Run the watchdog until SIGTERM or SIGINT.

    mode: policy.OBSERVE or policy.ENFORCE.
    restart_classes: the classes enforce mode may act on.
    process_source: "supervisor" or "proc".
    files: where the state goes.
    status_history: see Watchdog.

    return: 0; an unexpected error of the cycle propagates, so that
        supervisord restarts the watchdog.

    """
    wall = time.time()
    log_address = rpc_address("LogService", 0)
    alerter = Alerter(None if log_address is None else probes.RpcProbe(
        log_address[0], log_address[1], policy.LOG_ALERT_TIMEOUT_S))
    ledger, problem = files.load_ledger(wall)
    if problem:
        alerter.emit("ERROR", problem, wall)
    source = (control.SupervisorSource() if process_source == "supervisor"
              else control.ProcSource())
    dry_run = control.DryRunActuator(source, usr1_wait_s=policy.USR1_WAIT_S)
    actuator = control.SignalActuator(
        source, term_wait_s=policy.TERM_WAIT_S, confirm_s=policy.CONFIRM_S,
        usr1_wait_s=policy.USR1_WAIT_S) if source.can_act else dry_run
    prober = Prober(_gate_environment())
    rankings = [target for target in map(probes.ranking_target,
                                         config.proxy_service.rankings)
                if target is not None]
    watchdog = Watchdog(
        state=policy.PolicyState(mode=mode, restart_classes=restart_classes,
                                 ledger=ledger),
        source=source, actuator=actuator, dry_run=dry_run, prober=prober,
        files=files, alerter=alerter, rankings=rankings,
        rearm=lambda: faulthandler.dump_traceback_later(
            policy.SELF_GUARD_S, exit=True),
        status_history=status_history)
    alerter.emit("INFO", "Watchdog: started in %s mode%s, with the %s "
                 "source; it acts on nothing in its first %d s." % (
                     mode, " (it may restart %s)" % ",".join(
                         sorted(restart_classes))
                     if mode == policy.ENFORCE else "", process_source,
                     policy.GRACE_S), wall)
    loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
    stop = asyncio.Event()
    for signum in (signal.SIGTERM, signal.SIGINT):
        loop.add_signal_handler(signum, stop.set)
    runner = asyncio.create_task(watchdog.run_forever())
    stopper = asyncio.create_task(stop.wait())
    await asyncio.wait({runner, stopper},
                       return_when=asyncio.FIRST_COMPLETED)
    faulthandler.cancel_dump_traceback_later()
    stopper.cancel()
    prober.close()
    if runner.done():
        runner.result()
    runner.cancel()
    return 0


async def take_snapshot(program: str, files: Files,
                        source: control.ProcessSource | None = None,
                        prober: Prober | None = None,
                        out: TextIO | None = None,
                        rpc_address: AddressOf = rpc_address) -> int:
    """Write the evidence of a RUNNING program, and USR1 it (3.8).

    It books nothing and restarts nothing: run it before a manual
    restart. The pidfd check of an action applies.

    program: its supervisord name (as in ./status.sh), or its label.
    files: where the evidence goes.
    source, prober, out, rpc_address: tests pass fakes.

    return: 0, or 1 if no evidence was written.

    """
    out = out if out is not None else sys.stdout
    source = source if source is not None else control.SupervisorSource()
    try:
        facts = [policy.ProgramFacts(**asdict(info))
                 for info in await source.programs()]
    except Exception as error:
        print("cmsWatchdog: supervisord did not answer: %s"
              % probes.describe(error), file=out)
        return 1
    found = next((item for item in facts if item.name == program
                  or (item.managed and item.label == program)), None)
    if found is None or not found.managed or found.state != "RUNNING":
        why = ("it is unknown" if found is None
               else "it is not a CMS service" if not found.managed
               else "it is %s" % found.state)
        print("cmsWatchdog: no RUNNING CMS program named %r (%s). The CMS "
              "programs: %s." % (program, why, ", ".join(sorted(
                  item.name for item in facts if item.managed))), file=out)
        return 1
    own_prober = prober is None
    prober = prober if prober is not None else Prober(_gate_environment())
    try:
        gate = await prober.gate()
        address = (rpc_address(found.kind, found.shard)
                   if found.kind in policy.HEALTH_KINDS else None)
        health = (await prober.rpc(address, "health")
                  if address is not None else None)
        wall = time.time()
        name = files.evidence_name(wall, found.name)
        evidence = {
            "time": utc(wall), "taken_by": "cmsWatchdog --snapshot",
            "supervisor": asdict(found),
            "health": health.data if health is not None and health.ok
            else None,
            "health_error": health.error if health is not None else "",
            "gate": {"green": gate.green, "reason": gate.reason,
                     "detail": gate.detail},
            "snapshot": list(gate.backends),
            "proc": prober.proc_facts(found.pid),
        }
        outcome = await control.DryRunActuator(
            source, usr1_wait_s=policy.USR1_WAIT_S).snapshot(
            control.Target(found.name, found.kind, found.pid),
            lambda: files.write_evidence(name, evidence))
    finally:
        if own_prober:
            prober.close()
    if outcome.result != "evidence":
        print("cmsWatchdog: no evidence: %s." % outcome.detail, file=out)
        return 1
    if outcome.evidence_error:
        print("cmsWatchdog: the evidence could not be written: %s."
              % outcome.evidence_error, file=out)
        return 1
    print("Evidence: %s (%s)." % (os.path.join(files.evidence_dir, name),
                                  outcome.usr1), file=out)
    return 0


def parse_classes(text: str) -> frozenset[str]:
    """Read --restart: a comma-separated subset of the classes.

    raise (ValueError): for an unknown class.

    """
    classes = {item.strip().lower() for item in text.split(",")
               if item.strip()}
    unknown = classes - set(policy.CLASS_ORDER)
    if unknown:
        raise ValueError("unknown classes %s (choose from %s)" % (
            ",".join(sorted(unknown)), ",".join(policy.CLASS_ORDER)))
    return frozenset(classes)


def main(argv: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> int:
    """Run cmsWatchdog (see the module's docstring).

    argv: the arguments (sys.argv[1:] by default).

    return: the exit status.

    """
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        prog="cmsWatchdog",
        description="Restart a CMS program that is alive but hung (#38).")
    one_shot = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()
    one_shot.add_argument(
        "--status", action="store_true",
        help="print a summary of the status file, then exit")
    one_shot.add_argument(
        "--snapshot", metavar="PROGRAM",
        help="write the evidence of a RUNNING program (its name in "
             "./status.sh) and send it USR1 when that is safe, then exit; "
             "nothing is restarted")
    parser.add_argument(
        "--mode", choices=(policy.OBSERVE, policy.ENFORCE),
        default=policy.OBSERVE,
        help="observe logs what it would do; enforce does it")
    parser.add_argument(
        "--restart", default="web,core,infra", metavar="CLASSES",
        help="the classes enforce may restart, comma-separated, from web, "
             "core, infra and workers (default: web,core,infra)")
    parser.add_argument(
        "--process-source", choices=("supervisor", "proc"),
        default="supervisor",
        help="where the programs are found; proc (the load-test harness) "
             "cannot act and forces observe mode")
    parser.add_argument(
        "--status-history", metavar="PATH",
        help="also append each cycle's status to PATH as a JSON line")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    files = Files(config.global_.run_dir, config.global_.log_dir)
    if args.status:
        print(status_line(files.status, os.environ, time.time()))
        return 0
    if args.snapshot is not None:
        return asyncio.run(take_snapshot(args.snapshot, files))
    try:
        restart_classes = parse_classes(args.restart)
    except ValueError as error:
        parser.error(str(error))
    mode = args.mode
    if args.process_source == "proc" and mode == policy.ENFORCE:
        print("cmsWatchdog: the proc source cannot act, so the mode is "
              "observe.", file=sys.stderr)
        mode = policy.OBSERVE
    return asyncio.run(serve(mode, restart_classes, args.process_source,
                             files, args.status_history))
```

```python
def _watchdog(monkeypatch, extra=None):
    for name in ("CMS_WATCHDOG", "CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART"):
        monkeypatch.delenv(name, raising=False)
    _set(monkeypatch, extra)
    return _supervisord_programs(gc.generate_supervisord_conf()).get(
        "cmswatchdog")


def test_supervisord_watchdog_observes_by_default(monkeypatch):
    program = _watchdog(monkeypatch)
    assert program["command"] == \
        "cmsWatchdog --mode observe --restart web,core,infra"
    assert program["priority"] == "90"
    assert program["stopwaitsecs"] == "5"
    assert program["startretries"] == "30"
    assert program["stopasgroup"] == "true"
    assert program["killasgroup"] == "true"


def test_supervisord_watchdog_enforce(monkeypatch):
    program = _watchdog(monkeypatch, {
        "CMS_WATCHDOG": "enforce",
        "CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART": "web,core,infra,workers"})
    assert program["command"] == \
        "cmsWatchdog --mode enforce --restart web,core,infra,workers"


def test_supervisord_watchdog_off(monkeypatch):
    assert _watchdog(monkeypatch, {"CMS_WATCHDOG": "off"}) is None


def test_watchdog_knobs_are_normalized(monkeypatch):
    program = _watchdog(monkeypatch, {
        "CMS_WATCHDOG": " Enforce ",
        "CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART": "Web, core,,web,"})
    assert program["command"] == \
        "cmsWatchdog --mode enforce --restart web,core"


def test_watchdog_restart_empty_means_the_default(monkeypatch):
    program = _watchdog(monkeypatch, {"CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART": ""})
    assert program["command"].endswith("--restart web,core,infra")

    {"CMS_WATCHDOG": "on"},
    {"CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART": "web,db"},
    {"CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART": " , "},
])
def test_watchdog_invalid_knobs_exit(monkeypatch, capsys, variables):
    for name in ("CMS_WATCHDOG", "CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART"):
        monkeypatch.delenv(name, raising=False)
    _set(monkeypatch, variables)
    with pytest.raises(SystemExit):
        gc.generate_supervisord_conf()
    name = next(iter(variables))
    assert name in capsys.readouterr().err
```

```python
    result = run_status(FAKE_LOG=str(log), FAKE_WATCHDOG_LINE=(
        "watchdog: observe, last cycle 4 s ago, 0 open alerts"))
```

```python
def watchdog_settings() -> tuple[str, str]:
    """Read CMS_WATCHDOG and CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART; exit on a bad value.

    Case and spaces do not matter and a class given twice counts once.
    An empty value means the default, as for the other variables.
    """
    raw_mode = _get("CMS_WATCHDOG", "observe")
    mode = raw_mode.strip().lower()
    if mode not in WATCHDOG_MODES:
        print(f"ERROR: CMS_WATCHDOG must be off, observe or enforce, got "
              f"{raw_mode!r}.", file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(1)
    raw_classes = _get("CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART", "web,core,infra")
    classes: list[str] = []
    for item in raw_classes.split(","):
        item = item.strip().lower()
        if item and item not in classes:
            classes.append(item)
    if not classes or any(item not in WATCHDOG_CLASSES for item in classes):
        print(f"ERROR: CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART must be a comma-separated "
              f"subset of {','.join(WATCHDOG_CLASSES)}, got "
              f"{raw_classes!r}.", file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(1)
    return mode, ",".join(classes)
```

  - In `generate_supervisord_conf()`, right after
    `contest_id = _require("CMS_CONTEST_ID")`, add
    `watchdog_mode, watchdog_restart = watchdog_settings()`, and before
    `return "\n".join(blocks)` add:

```python
    if watchdog_mode != "off":
        # Priority 90: it starts last and stops first. Its own stop is
        # quick, so it gets 5 s (the stop_grace_period counts them).
        blocks.append(program(
            "cmswatchdog",
            f"cmsWatchdog --mode {watchdog_mode} "
            f"--restart {watchdog_restart}",
            90, stop_wait_secs=5))
```

~~~~markdown
## The watchdog

`cmsWatchdog` runs in the `cms` container, next to the CMS services. Every 15
seconds it checks that each one answers, and it can restart a program that is
alive but hung: a Contest Web Server that stopped answering, an Evaluation
Service stuck in a locked section, a Worker the Evaluation Service gave up on.
It restarts only that program, never the container, and never while the
database does not answer or while several programs fail together. After a
database outage, it also starts the programs that the outage left `FATAL`.
"When the watchdog alerts" in [Contest day](contest-day.md) says what its
alerts mean and how to turn it off.

Two variables of `.env` set it up; apply a change with `./up.sh`:

| Variable | What it does |
|----------|--------------|
| `CMS_WATCHDOG` | `observe` (the default): it logs what it would do (`WOULD restart …`) and restarts nothing. `enforce`: it restarts. `off`: it does not run. |
| `CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART` | The classes that `enforce` may restart, comma-separated: `web` (Contest and Admin Web Servers), `core` (Evaluation, Scoring and Proxy services), `infra` (Log and Resource services) and `workers`. The default, also when empty, is `web,core,infra`. Set it in the same edit that sets `CMS_WATCHDOG=enforce`, with only the classes that passed their checks (see "When the watchdog alerts"). |

`./status.sh` ends with the watchdog's line, for example
`watchdog: observe, last cycle 4 s ago, 0 open alerts`.

### With an external PostgreSQL

The watchdog reads `pg_stat_activity` to tell a hung program from one that
waits for a database lock. On an external PostgreSQL where the CMS role is not
a superuser, the rows of other roles' sessions (an operator's `psql`, for
example) hide most of their fields, and the watchdog's alerts show them as
`unknown`. To let it see them, run this once as a superuser, with the role of
`CMS_DB_URL` instead of `cms`:

```sql
GRANT pg_read_all_stats TO cms;
```

## Health checks in the reverse proxy

With several Contest Web Server processes, the reverse proxy should stop
sending users to a process that does not answer, and send them back once it
answers again. Caddy's defaults do not: one failed check ejects a process for
30 seconds, and with `lb_policy cookie` a user stays pinned to a dead process.
Until this is applied, even a restart of one process by hand gives its users
errors (502) for up to 30 seconds. This profile, tested on Caddy 2.11.7, moves
users off a hung process within about 35 seconds. In the site of the Contest
Web Server (keep your other options), list after `reverse_proxy` the port of
every Contest Web Server process: `CMS_CWS_HTTP_PORT` (8888 by default) for
process 0, then one more for each next process, up to
`CMS_CWS_HTTP_PORT + CMS_CWS_COUNT - 1`. Never list the port of the Admin Web
Server (`CMS_AWS_HTTP_PORT`):

```
reverse_proxy 127.0.0.1:8888 127.0.0.1:<port of process 1> {
    lb_policy cookie
    lb_try_duration 15s
    lb_try_interval 250ms

    health_uri /static/favicon.ico
    health_interval 10s
    health_timeout 5s
    health_fails 3
    health_passes 1

    fail_duration 10s
    max_fails 1

    transport http {
        dial_timeout 3s
        response_header_timeout 120s
    }
}
```

For the Admin Web Server, set only `lb_try_duration 15s`,
`lb_try_interval 250ms` and `dial_timeout 3s` (in `transport http`): no health
checks and no `response_header_timeout`, because exports can be slow and there
is no other process to send the request to.

Why these values:

- A process is ejected after 25 to 35 seconds without an answer, far above the
  2 to 3 seconds that a busy commit can freeze it.
- `max_fails 1` (the passive check) is what makes the cookie policy move a
  pinned user off a dead process at once.
- `response_header_timeout` must exceed the database pool timeout (60 seconds)
  plus the time of a handler.
- Do not set `unhealthy_status`, `unhealthy_latency`,
  `unhealthy_request_count` or `lb_retry_match`. By default Caddy retries only
  GET requests, which is right: a POST (a submission) may already have been
  saved.

Optionally, show a page for 502 and 503 with `handle_errors`, for example:
"The server is busy. Wait a minute and reload. Submissions already sent are
saved: check your list before sending again."

To apply it:

1. Back up the site file.
2. Run `caddy validate` on it, then reload Caddy gracefully.
3. Check that the admin API's `/reverse_proxy/upstreams` shows `fails: 0` for
   every process.
4. Never on a contest day, and never in the same window as a CMS deploy.

Revisit it when the lock limits of #39 stage 2 land: then `health_fails 5`,
and maybe a `response_header_timeout` of 180 seconds.
~~~~

~~~~markdown
## When the watchdog alerts

in [docker-deployment.md](docker-deployment.md)). In `./logs.sh cms` its lines
read `<UTC time> <LEVEL> Watchdog: …`, or `<UTC time> <LEVEL> Resolved: …`
when a problem is over; its warnings and errors also appear in the **Logs**
table on **Overview**, from `Watchdog,0`. `./status.sh` ends with its state. In
`observe` mode it restarts nothing: its `WOULD restart` lines say what
`enforce` would have done.

### What its lines mean

| Line | What it means | What to do |
|------|---------------|------------|
| `started in … mode …` | The watchdog started (also each time the container starts). It acts on nothing in its first 120 seconds. | Nothing. |
| `early warning: …` | A program missed 3 of its last 6 checks, or one of its sections is past half its limit. | Watch it; read `./logs.sh cms`. The line says what the watchdog does at the limit, in this mode, and which hold applies now. |
| `restarted … (pid A→B) after …` | It restarted a hung program: TERM, then KILL after 10 seconds if needed. | Read the evidence file it names, and the stacks in `./logs.sh cms`. For a Contest Web Server, see "When a Contest Web Server restarts". |
| `WOULD restart … after …` | In `observe` mode, or for a class that is not in `CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART`, it would have restarted the program. | Decide by hand, after `cmsWatchdog --snapshot` (see "The evidence"). |
| `… answers again N s after the restart` | The restart helped: the program passes its checks again. | Nothing. |
| `… is still failing 120 s after the restart` | The restart did not help. | Read the evidence and the logs; restart again by hand only once the cause is known. |
| `sent TERM … but no new pid was RUNNING within 30 s` | It signalled the program, but supervisord did not show it `RUNNING` again within 30 seconds. | Check `./status.sh`. If the program is `FATAL`, read `./logs.sh cms`, fix the cause, then start it with `supervisorctl`. |
| `did not restart …; nothing was signalled` | Between its check and the restart, the program exited or got another pid, so it did nothing. | Nothing, if `./status.sh` shows it `RUNNING`. |
| `did not restart …: its ledger could not be written …` | It could not record the restart in its ledger, so it did nothing: an unrecorded restart would escape the budget. | Check the free space of the container (the ledger is in its run directory); until the ledger can be written, it restarts nothing. |
| `… fails R… (…) but is held by H…` or `N Workers trip R4 together (…) and are held by H4` | It would restart the program (or start it, for R5), but a hold stops it. | Follow the row of that hold in "The holds". |
| `the database gate is red (…)` | PostgreSQL does not answer, or has no free connection; nothing is restarted meanwhile. | Follow "When the database misbehaves". |
| `… is FATAL and the database answered when it last started` | A program stopped for a reason other than a database outage. | Read `./logs.sh cms`, fix the cause, then start it with `supervisorctl`. |
| `started …, left FATAL by a database outage` | After an outage, it started the programs the outage had left `FATAL`. | Check `./status.sh`. |
| `WOULD start …, left FATAL by a database outage` | In `observe` mode, or for a class that is not in `CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART`, it would have started them. | Check `./status.sh`, and start them with `supervisorctl`. |
| `could not start all of …` or `did not start …: its ledger could not be written …` | supervisord did not start some of them, or the watchdog could not record the batch in its ledger and started nothing. | Read `./logs.sh cms`, then start them with `supervisorctl`. |
| `re-evaluation still running for N min` | A re-evaluation holds the Evaluation Service's lock; it is never restarted for that. | Nothing, unless new submissions must be graded first. |
| `ranking … does not answer` | A ranking does not answer; the watchdog cannot restart it. | Check the ranking container with `./status.sh` and `./logs.sh ranking`. |
| `pid N answers on the RPC port of …` | Another process answers on the program's RPC port, not the one supervisord started. The watchdog does nothing about it. | Check `./status.sh` and `ps` in the `cms` container, and end the stray process by hand. |
| `Resolved: …` | A problem it alerted about is over: the program passes its checks again, the database answers again, or the ranking answers again. | Nothing. |
| `the evidence of … could not be written`, or `its journal …`, `its status file …` or `its status history … could not be written` | A file of the watchdog could not be written (a full disk, for example); its restarts go on. | Check the free space of the disk that holds the file it names. |
| `its ledger … could not be written` | Its ledger could not be written at the end of a cycle: a restart stops at its booking until it can be. | Check the free space of the container (the ledger is in its run directory); until the ledger can be written, it restarts nothing. |
| `its ledger … did not parse (…)` | At its start, its ledger could not be read: every restart budget counts as used for 60 minutes. | Nothing; restart by hand if needed, after `cmsWatchdog --snapshot`. |
| `the restart of … failed unexpectedly: …` or `the start of … failed unexpectedly: …` | An unexpected error stopped one of its actions. | Check `./status.sh`, and keep the lines around it for the developers. |
| `paused for N s` | The watchdog itself was paused (the host, the VM or the container). | Nothing, if it happens once. |
| `… is gone: no process runs it any more …` | Only in the load-test harness, which reads `/proc` instead of supervisord. | Nothing. |
| `hourly near-miss summary` | How close each check came to its limit in the last hour. | Nothing; it is the record for switching `enforce` on. |

### The holds

A hold stops a restart and leaves an alert. The alert names the first hold
that applies (`held by H…`) and lists the others. While the hold lasts, the line
repeats every 5 minutes with the hold that applies then; the first time H7 or H3
applies, it goes out at once.

| Hold | It applies when | What to do |
|------|-----------------|------------|
| H1 | supervisord did not answer. | Run `./status.sh`. |
| H2 | The database does not answer, or has answered for less than 120 seconds. | Follow "When the database misbehaves". |
| H3 | A CMS session has waited more than 30 seconds for a database lock. The alert names up to 3 waiting sessions and up to 3 blockers of each: its pid, its program, its state and how long its transaction has been open. | See "A lock blocks CMS". Ending a blocker is your decision. |
| H4 | Two or more programs of the same class fail together (two Contest Web Servers, for example). | Find what they share (the database, the disk, the load). Restart them by hand one at a time, and only once you know the cause. |
| H5 | More than a third of the programs it probes fail a check at once (the Workers are not counted). | Check the load of the server and of the database. |
| H6 | The program, or the watchdog itself, started less than 120 seconds ago, or the watchdog acted on it less than 120 seconds ago. | Nothing: wait. |
| H7 | Its budget is used: 2 restarts of a program, or 3 of its pool, per hour. The alert says until when (`will not restart … before HH:MM UTC`). | Restart it by hand if needed, after `cmsWatchdog --snapshot`. |
| H8 | Another restart is still running. | Nothing. |

### A lock blocks CMS

An H3 alert names the session that holds the lock, for example
`pid 900 ('psql', idle in transaction, transaction open 300 s)`. If it is a
session you know (an operator's `psql`, a long export), let it finish or end
it. Find it with the holder query of "When the database misbehaves", and end
it with `SELECT pg_terminate_backend(<pid>);` the same way.

After a restart, a blocker marked `orphan of pid N` is the database session of
the process the watchdog killed (pid N): its connection is gone, but
PostgreSQL may keep its transaction and its locks. End it with
`pg_terminate_backend` as above. End only a blocker marked that way: an
unmarked blocker with the same program name may be the new process's own
session.

### The evidence

Before every restart and every `WOULD restart`, the watchdog writes a file in
the `watchdog/` directory of the CMS log volume (`log/watchdog/` in its
alerts): the decision, the program's last 6 checks, the last `health()`
answer it received from the program (with the stack of a stuck section), the
database sessions and the state of the process. It also makes the program write the stack of each of its threads
to `./logs.sh cms` (lines that start with `Thread 0x` and
`Current thread 0x`), unless the program is stopped. `actions.jsonl`, in the
same directory, lists every action with its time. To list them:

```bash
docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.prod.yml --env-file .env -p <project> \
    exec -T cms ls -l /home/cmsuser/cms/log/watchdog
```

Before you restart a program by hand, keep the same evidence with:

```bash
docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.prod.yml --env-file .env -p <project> \
    exec -T cms cmsWatchdog --snapshot <program>
```

`<program>` is the name that `./status.sh` shows, for example
`cmscontestwebserver2`. It restarts nothing, and it prints the file it wrote.

### When a Contest Web Server restarts

- With the Caddy profile of "Health checks in the reverse proxy" in
  [docker-deployment.md](docker-deployment.md), its users move to another
  process within about 35 seconds; without it, they get errors for up to 30
  seconds after the restart.
- A submission cut off by the restart may have been saved: tell the
  contestants to check their list of submissions before they send it again.
- Up to 60 seconds of participant activity of that process are lost. The
  restart times, for the review of simultaneous activity, are in
  `actions.jsonl`.
- If every process answers 503 with `no upstreams`, it heals by itself within
  about 30 seconds: restart nothing.

### Turning it off

- **Until the container restarts**, with no downtime:

  ```bash
  docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.prod.yml --env-file .env -p <project> \
      exec cms supervisorctl -c /home/cmsuser/cms/etc/supervisord.conf stop cmswatchdog
  ```

- **For good:** set `CMS_WATCHDOG=off` in `.env`, then run `./up.sh`; it
  recreates the containers, so every service restarts.

### Switching enforce on

Only between contests (never on a contest day, nor in the 3 days before one),
and only for the classes that passed all of these in `observe` mode:

- every drill of the watchdog's validation passed on the deployed version;
- at least one session with real users (a contest, or a rehearsal of 2 hours
  or more) ran in `observe` mode;
- every `WOULD restart` line is explained, and no H4 or H5 alert appeared
  outside known database events;
- the hourly summaries stayed under half of each limit: at most 2 misses in 6
  checks, no Evaluation Service section older than 90 seconds with no
  statement for 30 seconds (re-evaluations aside), no scoring longer than 90
  seconds, and no ranking send idle for 90 seconds;
- the nightly 05:00 restart was seen at least 7 times;
- you ran the game day (below).

Then, in one edit of `.env`, set `CMS_WATCHDOG=enforce` and set
`CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART` to only those classes (for example
`CMS_WATCHDOG_RESTART=web`), run `./up.sh`, and run the game day again. Add
`workers` later, once `observe` mode has shown no false Worker alert.

### The game day

In a quiet window and never on a contest day: once in `observe` mode, and once
right after switching `enforce` on.

1. Stop a Contest Web Server for 120 seconds, then let it go on (here process
   3; use one that exists):

   ```bash
   docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.prod.yml --env-file .env -p <project> \
       exec -T cms bash -c 'pid=$(supervisorctl -c /home/cmsuser/cms/etc/supervisord.conf pid cmscontestwebserver3); kill -STOP "$pid"; sleep 120; kill -CONT "$pid"'
   ```

2. Do the same with the Scoring Service (`cmsscoringservice`) for 240
   seconds.
3. Check the reverse proxy's log, the **Logs** table on **Overview**,
   `./logs.sh cms` and the evidence directory: an early warning, then
   `WOULD restart` (`restarted` in `enforce` mode), and no line about the
   other programs.
~~~~

```markdown
| `Watchdog: restarted …` or `Watchdog: WOULD restart …` | Read the evidence; tell the contestants of a restarted Contest Web Server to check their submissions | "When the watchdog alerts" (above) |
| `./status.sh` shows `no status file`, or a watchdog line whose last cycle is minutes old | The watchdog is not running: check its row (`cmswatchdog`) in `./status.sh` and read `./logs.sh cms` | "When the watchdog alerts" (above) |
```

  | Its budget is used: 2 restarts of a program, or 3 of its pool, per hour. The alert says until when (`will not restart … before HH:MM UTC`). | Su presupuesto está agotado: 2 reinicios de un programa, o 3 de su grupo, por hora. La alerta dice hasta cuándo (`will not restart … before HH:MM UTC`). |
  | Restart it by hand if needed, after `cmsWatchdog --snapshot`. | Reinícialo a mano si hace falta, después de `cmsWatchdog --snapshot`. |
  | Another restart is still running. | Otro reinicio sigue en curso. |
  | Nothing. | Nada. |
  | A lock blocks CMS | Un bloqueo detiene a CMS |
  | An H3 alert names the session that holds the lock, for example `pid 900 ('psql', idle in transaction, transaction open 300 s)`. If it is a session you know (an operator's `psql`, a long export), let it finish or end it. Find it with the holder query of "When the database misbehaves", and end it with `SELECT pg_terminate_backend(<pid>);` the same way. | Una alerta H3 nombra la sesión que tiene el bloqueo, por ejemplo `pid 900 ('psql', idle in transaction, transaction open 300 s)`. Si es una sesión que conoces (el `psql` de un operador, una exportación larga), deja que termine o termínala. Encuéntrala con la consulta de sesiones abiertas de "Cuando la base de datos falla", y termínala con `SELECT pg_terminate_backend(<pid>);` de la misma forma. |
  | After a restart, a blocker marked `orphan of pid N` is the database session of the process the watchdog killed (pid N): its connection is gone, but PostgreSQL may keep its transaction and its locks. End it with `pg_terminate_backend` as above. End only a blocker marked that way: an unmarked blocker with the same program name may be the new process's own session. | Después de un reinicio, una sesión que bloquea marcada `orphan of pid N` es la sesión de base de datos del proceso que el watchdog mató (pid N): su conexión ya no existe, pero PostgreSQL puede conservar su transacción y sus bloqueos. Termínala con `pg_terminate_backend` como arriba. Termina solo una sesión marcada así: una sin marca con el mismo nombre de programa puede ser la sesión del proceso nuevo. |

It also makes the program write the stack of each of its threads to `./logs.sh cms` (lines that start with `Thread 0x` and `Current thread 0x`), unless the program is stopped. `actions.jsonl`, in the same directory, lists every action with its time. También hace que el programa escriba la pila de cada uno de sus hilos en `./logs.sh cms` (líneas que empiezan con `Thread 0x` y `Current thread 0x`), salvo que el programa esté detenido. `actions.jsonl`, en el mismo directorio, lista cada acción con su hora. class ClientDropTest(unittest.TestCase):
```python
    """The single-connection drops of the watchdog's drills (#38)."""

    setUp = InjectorTest.setUp
    _injector = InjectorTest._injector

    def test_the_rules_cut_one_client_port_both_ways(self):
        self.assertEqual(faults.client_port_rules(51234), (
            ("INPUT", "!", "-i", "lo", "-p", "tcp", "--sport", "51234",
             "--dport", "5432", "-j", "DROP"),
            ("OUTPUT", "!", "-o", "lo", "-p", "tcp", "--sport", "5432",
             "--dport", "51234", "-j", "DROP")))

    def test_the_drop_goes_in_and_comes_out(self):
        injector = self._injector()
        started = self.time.now
        injector.drop_client_ports([51234, 51236], 300)
        rules = [rule for port in (51234, 51236)
                 for rule in faults.client_port_rules(port)]
        self.assertEqual(
            [command for command in self.runner.commands
             if command[0] == "sudo"],
            [faults.iptables_argv(4242, "-I", rule) for rule in rules]
            + [faults.iptables_argv(4242, "-D", rule)
               for rule in reversed(rules)])
        self.assertEqual(injector.records[-1], {
            "fault": "client-drop", "ports": [51234, 51236],
            "start": started, "end": started + 300,
            "rules_removed": True})
        self.assertEqual(injector.rules, [])
        with open(self.out) as f:
            self.assertEqual(json.load(f)[-1]["fault"], "client-drop")

    def test_an_interrupted_drop_still_removes_its_rules(self):
        injector = self._injector(time_source=FakeTime(interrupt_at=300))
        with self.assertRaises(SystemExit):
            injector.drop_client_ports([51234], 300)
        self.assertEqual(
            [command for command in self.runner.commands
             if command[0] == "sudo" and "-D" in command],
            [faults.iptables_argv(4242, "-D", rule)
             for rule in reversed(faults.client_port_rules(51234))])
        self.assertEqual(injector.rules, [])
        self.assertIn("end", injector.records[-1])

    def test_the_command_line(self):
        argv = ["faults.py", "--project", "p", "--compose-file", "c",
                "--env-file", "e", "--out", self.out,
                "--drop-client-ports", "51234,51236", "--max-s", "5"]
        with patch.object(sys, "argv", argv), \
                patch.object(faults, "Injector") as injector_class, \
                patch.object(faults.signal, "signal"):
            self.assertEqual(faults.main(), 0)
        injector = injector_class.return_value
        injector.drop_client_ports.assert_called_once_with([51234, 51236],
                                                           5.0)
        injector.run_schedule.assert_not_called()
        injector.cleanup.assert_called_once_with()

    def test_the_schedule_still_needs_the_users_file(self):
        argv = ["faults.py", "--project", "p", "--compose-file", "c",
                "--env-file", "e", "--out", self.out]
        with patch.object(sys, "argv", argv), \
                contextlib.redirect_stderr(io.StringIO()), \
                self.assertRaises(SystemExit):
            faults.main()
```

```python


def client_port_rules(client_port: int) -> tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...]:
    """Return the DROP rules of one client connection to PostgreSQL.

    In PostgreSQL's network namespace: what arrives from that client
    port, and what leaves to it, except on the loopback. They cut one
    connection of a program and leave its other connections, and every
    other program, alone (the watchdog's drills D7 and D15, #38).

    client_port: the connection's client_port in pg_stat_activity.

    return: the rules, for iptables_argv.

    """
    port = str(client_port)
    return (
        ("INPUT", "!", "-i", "lo", "-p", "tcp", "--sport", port,
         "--dport", "5432", "-j", "DROP"),
        ("OUTPUT", "!", "-o", "lo", "-p", "tcp", "--sport", "5432",
         "--dport", port, "-j", "DROP"),
    )
```

  - In `Injector`, after `f4`, add:

```python
    def drop_client_ports(self, ports: Sequence[int], max_s: float) -> None:
        """Drop the PostgreSQL connections of some client ports.

        The drop lasts max_s seconds, or until SIGTERM or Ctrl-C (a
        drill ends it right after the watchdog's restart is confirmed);
        either way the rules are removed and the record saved.

        ports: the client ports, from pg_stat_activity.
        max_s: how long the drop lasts at most.

        """
        pid = self.db_pid()
        record: dict = {"fault": "client-drop", "ports": list(ports),
                        "start": self.clock()}
        self.records.append(record)
        try:
            for port in ports:
                for rule in client_port_rules(port):
                    # Recorded before it goes in, as in f2.
                    self.rules.append((pid, rule))
                    self.run(iptables_argv(pid, "-I", rule))
            self.save()
            self.sleep(max_s)
        finally:
            record["end"] = self.clock()
            record["rules_removed"] = self._delete_rules()
            self.save()
```

  - Replace `main()` with:

```python
def _client_ports(text: str) -> list[int]:
    """Read --drop-client-ports: comma-separated port numbers."""
    try:
        ports = [int(port) for port in text.split(",") if port.strip()]
    except ValueError as error:
        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(str(error)) from error
    if not ports or not all(0 < port < 65536 for port in ports):
        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError("not a list of ports: %r" % text)
    return ports


def main() -> int:
    """Parse the arguments and inject the faults."""
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
    parser.add_argument("--project", required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--compose-file", required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--env-file", required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--users-file",
                        help="users.json, for the contest start")
    parser.add_argument("--out", required=True, help="faults.json")
    parser.add_argument(
        "--drop-client-ports", type=_client_ports, metavar="PORTS",
        help="instead of the schedule, drop the PostgreSQL connections "
             "of these comma-separated client ports")
    parser.add_argument("--max-s", type=float, default=300.0,
                        help="how long --drop-client-ports lasts at most")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.drop_client_ports is None and args.users_file is None:
        parser.error("--users-file is required without "
                     "--drop-client-ports")
    contest_start = None
    if args.drop_client_ports is None:
        with open(args.users_file) as f:
            contest_start = json.load(f)["start"]
    injector = Injector(args.project, args.compose_file, args.env_file,
                        args.out)
    # run.sh ends the injector with SIGTERM if the run ends first.
    signal.signal(signal.SIGTERM, lambda signum, frame: sys.exit(143))
    try:
        if args.drop_client_ports is not None:
            injector.drop_client_ports(args.drop_client_ports, args.max_s)
        else:
            injector.run_schedule(contest_start)
    finally:
        # A second SIGTERM must not cut the cleanup short.
        signal.signal(signal.SIGTERM, signal.SIG_IGN)
        injector.cleanup()
    return 0
```

```python
# (fork header)

"""Tests for the pass criteria of a watchdog run (watchdogstats.py)."""

import json
import os
import shutil
import sys
import tempfile
import time
import unittest

from cms.watchdog import policy

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(
    os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "..", "loadtest"))

import watchdogstats  # noqa: E402


START = 1_800_000_000.0
LABELS = ["cmsLogService 0", "cmsEvaluationService 0",
          "cmsScoringService 0", "cmsProxyService 0",
          "cmsContestWebServer 0", "cmsContestWebServer 1",
          "cmsAdminWebServer 0", "cmsWorker 0"]


def entry(label: str, **changes) -> dict:
    kind = label.split()[0][3:]
    probed = kind != "Worker"
    http = kind in ("ContestWebServer", "AdminWebServer")
    data = {"name": label, "label": label, "kind": kind,
            "state": "RUNNING", "pid": 100, "misses": 0, "known": 6,
            "http_ok": True if http else None,
            "http_latency_s": 0.02 if http else None,
            "rpc_ok": True if probed else None,
            "rpc_latency_s": 0.01 if probed else None, "sections": []}
    data.update(changes)
    return data


def status(index: int, labels=LABELS, gate: str = "green",
           changes: dict | None = None) -> dict:
    programs = [entry(label, **(changes or {}).get(label, {}))
                for label in labels]
    return {"time": START + 15 * index, "gate": gate, "programs": programs}


def log_line(when: float, text: str) -> str:
    return "%s WARNING Watchdog: %s" % (
        time.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S UTC", time.gmtime(when)), text)


class WatchdogStatsTest(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.run_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
        self.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, self.run_dir)
        self.statuses = [status(index) for index in range(40)]
        self.log: list[str] = []
        self.faults: list[dict] | None = None
        with open(os.path.join(self.run_dir, "requests.jsonl"), "w") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps({"t": START + 30, "dur": 0.1}) + "\n")

    def rows(self) -> dict[str, bool]:
        with open(os.path.join(self.run_dir, "watchdog_status.jsonl"),
                  "w") as f:
            for item in self.statuses:
                f.write(json.dumps(item) + "\n")
        with open(os.path.join(self.run_dir, "watchdog.log"), "w") as f:
            f.write("\n".join(self.log) + "\n")
        if self.faults is not None:
            with open(os.path.join(self.run_dir, "faults.json"), "w") as f:
                json.dump(self.faults, f)
        rows, _ = watchdogstats.check(self.run_dir, cws=2, workers=1)
        return {criterion: passed for criterion, _, _, passed in rows}

    def test_a_quiet_run_passes(self):
        rows = self.rows()
        self.assertTrue(all(rows.values()), rows)

    def test_a_would_line_fails(self):
        self.log.append(log_line(START + 100, "WOULD restart "
                                 "cmsContestWebServer 1 (pid 100) after"))
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["no WOULD line"])

    def test_an_early_warning_is_neither_a_hold_nor_a_would_line(self):
        # The policy's warnings name the hold that applies now and the
        # restart it would do, in other words than the held and WOULD
        # lines.
        self.log.append(log_line(
            START + 100, "early warning: 3 of the last 6 favicon probes "
            "of cmsContestWebServer 1 failed. At 5 misses, the latest "
            "included, the watchdog would restart it. But H2 applies now: "
            "the database gate is red (refused). Check ./logs.sh for its "
            "log; cmsWatchdog --snapshot cmscontestwebserver1 writes its "
            "stacks."))
        rows = self.rows()
        self.assertTrue(rows["no WOULD line"])
        self.assertTrue(rows["holds only inside F2 and F3"])

    def test_a_hold_fails_outside_a_fault_window_only(self):
        self.log.append(log_line(START + 300, "cmsContestWebServer 1 fails "
                                 "R1 (5 of 6) but is held by H2: x."))
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["holds only inside F2 and F3"])
        self.faults = [{"fault": "F2", "start": START + 250,
                        "end": START + 340}]
        self.assertTrue(self.rows()["holds only inside F2 and F3"])

    def test_a_red_gate_fails_outside_a_fault_window_only(self):
        self.statuses[20] = status(20, gate="red")
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["gate red only inside F2 and F3"])
        self.faults = [{"fault": "F3", "start": START + 300,
                        "end": START + 330},
                       {"fault": "F1", "start": START + 100,
                        "end": START + 101}]
        self.assertTrue(self.rows()["gate red only inside F2 and F3"])

    def test_programs_must_stay_from_the_driver_start(self):
        without_proxy = [label for label in LABELS
                         if label != "cmsProxyService 0"]
        # ProxyService starts late, after the driver's start: fine.
        for index in range(4):
            self.statuses[index] = status(index, labels=without_proxy)
        self.assertTrue(self.rows()["every program in every cycle"])
        # It disappears after it appeared: not fine.
        self.statuses[30] = status(30, labels=without_proxy)
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["every program in every cycle"])

    def test_a_program_that_never_appears_fails(self):
        self.rows()
        rows, _ = watchdogstats.check(self.run_dir, cws=3, workers=1)
        passed = {criterion: ok for criterion, _, _, ok in rows}
        self.assertFalse(passed["every program in every cycle"])

    def test_an_absent_program_fails(self):
        self.statuses[25] = status(25, changes={
            "cmsWorker 0": {"state": "ABSENT", "pid": None}})
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["every program in every cycle"])

    def test_slow_probes_fail(self):
        for index in range(0, 40, 5):
            self.statuses[index] = status(index, changes={
                "cmsContestWebServer 0": {"http_latency_s": 2.0}})
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["favicon p99 under 1/3 of 5 s"])

    def test_near_misses(self):
        self.statuses[10] = status(10, changes={
            "cmsContestWebServer 1": {"misses": 3}})
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["at most 2 misses in 6"])

    def test_sections_past_half_their_trip(self):
        es = "cmsEvaluationService 0"
        held_lock = {"what": "post_finish_lock:_write_results_sync",
                     "age_s": 95.0, "idle_s": 31.0, "tid": 1,
                     "statements": 3}
        self.statuses[10] = status(10, changes={es: {"sections": [
            held_lock]}})
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["ES sections under half their trip"])
        self.statuses[10] = status(10, changes={es: {"sections": [
            dict(held_lock, what=watchdogstats.INVALIDATION)]}})
        self.assertTrue(self.rows()["ES sections under half their trip"])

    def test_a_fault_run_records_the_latencies_and_near_misses(self):
        # L3: F2 and F3 may push them past half their trips by design.
        self.statuses[10] = status(10, changes={
            "cmsContestWebServer 1": {"misses": 3, "http_latency_s": 4.0}})
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["at most 2 misses in 6"])
        self.faults = [{"fault": "F2", "start": START + 100,
                        "end": START + 190}]
        rows = self.rows()
        self.assertTrue(rows["at most 2 misses in 6"])
        self.assertTrue(rows["favicon p99 under 1/3 of 5 s"])
        self.assertTrue(all(rows.values()), rows)
        # The L3 criteria still apply.
        self.log.append(log_line(START + 100, "WOULD restart "
                                 "cmsContestWebServer 1 (pid 100) after"))
        self.assertFalse(self.rows()["no WOULD line"])

    def test_the_constants_match_the_policy(self):
        self.assertEqual(watchdogstats.HTTP_TIMEOUT_S,
                         policy.HTTP_TIMEOUT_S)
        self.assertEqual(watchdogstats.RPC_TIMEOUT_S, policy.RPC_TIMEOUT_S)
        self.assertEqual(watchdogstats.HALF_MISSES,
                         policy.MISSES_TO_WARN - 1)
        self.assertEqual(watchdogstats.HALF_SECTION_S,
                         policy.SECTION_WARN_S)
        self.assertEqual(watchdogstats.HALF_ES_IDLE_S,
                         policy.ES_IDLE_WARN_S)
        self.assertEqual(watchdogstats.INVALIDATION,
                         policy.INVALIDATION_SECTION)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```
